| ps `c254`, `c255` | literals: the 1 of every `1 - x`, the 2 and -1 of the normal decode, the 0 of a `dp2add`, the slope threshold, the small term in `wx`, `wy`, a factor on `N . c109` | read by idiom at first; which register holds which differs per shader (`ps_check.py`, `LAYERS`). Session 1 (`"ps_lit"`, registers 252 to 255): `9C21`: `c254 = (2, 0.99609375, 8, 0)`, `c255 = (0, -1, 1, 0.0001)`; `04F2`: `c254` the same, `c255 = (0, 1, -1, 0.0001)`; `4524`: `c254 = (8, 2, 0.99609375, 0)`, `c255 = (0, 1, -1, 0.0001)`; `4755`: `(2, 0, -1, 1)`, `(8, 0, 0, 0)`; `AF1E`: `(0, 1, -1, 8)`, `(2, 0, 0, 0)`; `5783`: `(0, 1, -1, 2)`, `(8, 0, 0, 0)`; `7DDA` / `2F8A`: `c255 = (1, 0, 0, 0)`. The idiom reading holds; the slope threshold is 255/256 where it was read as 1 |

`c21.xyz` and `c20.xyz` change with the time of day like the sun direction, so `c21.xyz` is a light colour by behaviour, although it multiplies the base map. tf3 is baked light (session 1): the texture (`session1_tex_bridge\1B1E5052.png`, 768 x 768) is a pale blue-grey sheet (mean 133, 147, 158; standard deviation 12 to 21) with pale yellow-green areas and soft dark blotches in the shapes of building and object footprints with halos; it has no path and no grass or dirt colour. So `pow(tf3, c21.w) * c21.xyz`, which the spec's material kept, is light and occlusion under a blue ambient tint, not ground colour: the replay with it gives grey-blue dirt and cold grass, the replay without the tint is neutral and dark, and the replay of the detail colours alone has the emulated ground's hues, warm tan dirt and yellow-green grass (`session1_bridge_mosaic_sheet_default_notint_nobase.png`, `session1_bridge_patch_sheet_49_31_29_default_notint_nobase_nofade.png`). Ruling (2026-10-07): tf3 and `c21` belong to the lighting sub-project.

**The unlit rule** (ruling of 2026-10-07 after session 1; the rule of the first reading kept `pow(tf3, c21.w) * c21.xyz` and is withdrawn). The terrain material is the colour texture alone:

```
layer (terrain-layer):         colour = tf0 at (c8.y * x, -c8.y * y)      alpha = saturate(weight) * fade
  with the steep-ground block:  colour = sqrt(s * tf0^2 + (1 - s) * (wy * tf13(o1)^2 + wx * tf13(o0)^2)) where s < threshold
far (terrain-far, 7DDA):       colour = tf13 at the coordinate of the two tables      alpha = 1
underlay (terrain-far-fade, 2F8A):  colour = tf13 * sat(d2 * c31.x + c31.y)          alpha = 1
weight = c253.w * (tf18.w - c253.y)        fade = 1 - sat(d2 * c31.x + c31.y)
```

The colour is taken as stored. The shaders square it (`A = tf0^2`) in the game's linear space and the game's later passes bring the frame back to display values; the native target holds display values like every other textured draw, and the square root of `tf0^2` is `tf0`. Dropped: tf3 with its exponent and tint `c21` (baked light and an ambient tint: "Constants"); the lighting factor L, `c11.x` and the output scale `c20.w`; `c20.xyz`; every light term (tf8 mask, tf9 shadow, `c80..c83`, `c87..c90` and the cone and range constants); the fog (tf4, tf5, `c28`, `c66..c68`); the normal maps tf2 and tf14. `ps_check.py` shows the first reading's rule as the shader's own output with `c22 = 1`, `c20 = (0, 0, 0, 1)`, `c11.x = 1`, the light colours zero and the fog predicate off; the rule as ruled is that output with the tf3 term 1 and the square root taken. Three things the rule leaves, each decided or deferred:

- *Negative alpha.* A first layer whose texel is 0 has the weight -1/14, and the game's colour target is float (`RB_COLOR_INFO` formats 3 and 12, "Discovery rows"), so the game does not clamp it: such a pixel subtracts a fourteenth of the layer's colour. The native material saturates the weight to 0 (ruling). The two differ only at those texels.
- *Mixing in display space.* The game adds the layers up in its linear space, the native pass adds the stored colours. Where one layer has the whole weight (the weights are close to binary, "The weight cells") the two agree; in a transition band about one weight texel (half a world unit) wide the native mix is slightly darker than the game's, since the weighted mean of two values is at most the root of the weighted mean of their squares. Ruling: accepted; the exact fix is a linear intermediate target. (Since the lighting sub-project the clay target is linear and the layers add up as in the game: section 14, "Linear target".)
- *Steep ground* (ruling of 2026-10-07, plan Task 10b). For a layer of the three shaders with the block of item 1 the colour is the display value of the game's linear mix, computed per pixel: the square root of `s * tf0^2 + (1 - s) * (wy * tf13(o1)^2 + wx * tf13(o0)^2)` where s is below the threshold, and `tf0` elsewhere, with s, the weights and the two side coordinates as in item 1. Alpha, fade, cull and blending are unchanged, so the layers of a pixel are still added in display space (the point above; since the lighting sub-project the shader writes the linear mix itself, without the root: section 14, "Linear target"). About a third of the near ground at the bridge and by the trees is steep enough for the block (item 1).

**Layer order and blend state** (tess rows; `underlay_all_bridge_211654.txt`, `underlay_all_trees_211843.txt`, `layer_order_bridge_211654_f2.txt`, `underlay_bridge_211654_f2.txt`):

- Every frame of both captures has the same order in the main scene: mesh draws, all draws of `82207C30`, mesh draws, all draws of `8221C9C8`, mesh draws (24 of 24 frames each).
- Per frame at the bridge / by the trees: `82207C30` with `7DDA...` 57 / 11, with `2F8A...` 18 / 12; `8221C9C8` with `9C21...` 230 / 293, `4755...` 118 / 74, `4524...` 92 / 23, `AF1E...` 31 / 13, `04F2...` 18 / 18, `5783...` 3 / 5. Each of the three vertex shaders is paired only as in the tables above.
- A layer draw is one sub-patch (8 x 8 units) of one tile for one layer. The builder goes tile by tile, and within a tile layer by layer, each layer drawing those of the four sub-patches it covers. A square has 1 to 11 layer draws (most 4 to 7).
- The first layer draw of a square is either `0x00060006` (source alpha, zero) with `c114.x = -1`, and then no draw of the adaptive builder covers the square in a colour pass (648 squares in 24 frames at the bridge, 744 by the trees); or `0x01060106` (source alpha, one) with `c114.x` = 1/255, and then `2F8A...` covers it in a colour pass (1,560 and 1,656). No square is an exception, and every later layer draw is `0x01060106` with 1/255 (9,600 and 7,824).
- The squares without a colour underlay are still covered by a `2F8A...` draw whose bin mask is `0x15555555`: the depth-only draws of "Pre-pass draws and the depth range" (3 per frame at the bridge, 36 patches each). So those draws fill the depth for squares whose colour comes from the layers alone, which answers the question left open there. `7DDA...` covers no square that has a layer (0 of 1,224 squares of frame 2).
- The fade distances fit where the squares lie (the review's figures, not recomputed here): the squares without an underlay end 42.3 units from the eye at the bridge and 46.1 by the trees, inside the 48 units where the layers are whole, and the patches of the plain far shader `7DDA...` begin at 63.4 and 68.1, by the 64 units where the layers are gone.
- All layer draws: depth `0x00708767` (greater-equal, write), cull `0x00218002`, no alpha test, bin masks `0x0003000A` or `0x00030002`. Adaptive draws: `0x00708766`, blend off.

**Native terrain material** (plan Tasks 10 and 10b; `clay_logic.h` "Terrain material", `clay_pass.cpp` `PrepareTerrain` / `FinishTerrain`, `fable2_native_shaders.h`; the reference is `terrain_replay.py`, variant `--no-base`). The clay pass draws a terrain record with the unlit rule above when its inputs and textures are usable, and as flat clay otherwise.

- *Shaders.* Two pixel shaders beside the flat and the textured one, chosen by the builder: `kClayTerrainLayerPs` for a draw of `8221C9C8` and `kClayTerrainFarPs` for a draw of `82207C30` (a flag in the constants multiplies the far colour by the fade-in for the kind `terrain-far-fade`). The clay vertex shader hands the position it read to the pixel shader in a third interpolator; for a terrain record that is the world position (x, y, height) of the grid point. The layer shader forms the two coordinates, the weight, the cull and the distance per pixel from it. The weight cell is read with a linear filter whatever the fetch constant says (it is the vertex shader's, point sampled at texel centres): between the grid points the value is the bilinear mix of the game's vertex values. The cull is a `discard` where that value is below `c114.x`; the adaptive shaders have no cull and the far shader has none. Both shaders end in the textured shader's alpha test, alpha-to-mask and output, with the same softened facet shade (0.75 + 0.25 of the facet term), which is the same for every layer of a pixel. The HLSL holds each term in one statement; `test_native_shaders.cpp` pins the statements and their order.
- *State.* Every terrain draw keeps its captured state through the path of "Render state": the layers blend source alpha / one or source alpha / zero and write the shader's alpha, the adaptive draws are opaque. The sum in the target is `S * far + sum(fade * w_i * tf0_i)` in display values; the target is 8 bits per channel and clamps at 1, which the sum does not reach (the weights of a square add up to at most 1). (That was the materials sub-project. Since the lighting sub-project the target is a linear float target and the sum is the game's, `S * far^2 + sum(fade * w_i * tf0_i^2)`: section 14, "Linear target".) The layers pass the depth test at equal depth (greater-equal in the game, less-equal on the pass's depth). That needs equal depth values, and they are equal by construction: an underlay patch and the layers of the square above it are built from the same heightmap with the same height scale on the same 17 x 17 grid points, whose coordinates (multiples of half a unit added to integer origins) are exact in single precision, so both give the same positions, the same triangles and the same depth. No bias and no change to the depth test was needed; no z-fighting shows in any shot.
- *Constants and textures.* The terrain constants are cbuffer `Terrain` at `b1`, 160 bytes (128 before the steep-ground block): the two coordinate transforms, the three matrix rows, the eye, the cull reference, the fade pair, the two weight literals, three sampler numbers and the flags, and for the block the side scale, the slope pair and the two literals. They do not fit the root signature beside the draw's 36 words (the RHI has one block of root constants per layout), so each terrain draw of a frame has a 256-byte slot in one upload buffer bound as a constant buffer (root signature: 46 of 64 words). A buffer is in use until its frame's submission completes, so the pass takes one from a small pool each frame. `kClayTerrainFields` beside the HLSL is the layout; the tests hold it against the compiled shaders (reflection, both shaders) and against the C++ struct. Textures are a second table: `t4` the colour (tf0 or tf13), `t5` the weight cells and `t6` the side texture of a layer with the steep-ground block, with the pass's white texture bound for the weights of a far draw and for the side texture of every draw without the block. Samplers are the pass's four static ones, by each colour texture's own fetch constant: the detail textures are linear and wrap, the far atlases are point sampled and clamp. Each distinct set of the three takes three slots of the RHI's shader-visible heap while its views live. Before the steep-ground block there were 77 pairs per frame at the bridge (13 detail textures with the one weight atlas, 64 far atlases); with it a detail texture counts once per side texture it is drawn with (four side textures are in the bridge frame's rows), and the frame has 86 sets at the bridge, 33 by the trees and 89 at the third spot (the user-present runs of plan Task 11, "Validation").
- *Steep ground* (plan Task 10b). The layer shader evaluates the block of item 1 when the draw's constants say so (`t_flags` bit 1): there is no further pixel shader and no further pipeline. The side coordinates are formed per pixel from the world position the shader already has (`o0 = world.yz * (c8.x, -c8.x)`, `o1 = world.xz * (c8.x, -c8.x)`; they are linear in the position, so they are the game's interpolated ones). Their screen derivatives are taken for every pixel and the two fetches are made inside a real branch with those derivatives (`SampleGrad`; the compiled shader keeps the branch, `task10b\layer_ps_disassembly.txt`), so a flat pixel costs the slope value and no fetch. The mix is continuous where the block switches on: just below the threshold the side colour enters with at most 1/256. *The normal is per vertex.* `BuildTerrainNormals` (`terrain_patch.h`) reads the normal map at the height coordinate of every grid point, point sampled as the vertex shader's fetch is, and the geometry cache keeps `(n.x, n.y)` as the draw's buffer at `t2`, where a textured draw has its UVs (`TerrainNormals`; key `TerrainNormalKey`, kind 8, with the content hash of the map; the layers of a square share one buffer of 289 pairs). The clay vertex shader forms `z = sqrt(|1 - (x^2 + y^2)|)` as the game's does and hands the three on in a fourth interpolator ((0, 0, 1) for every other draw), so the pixel shader has the game's vertex normal interpolated over the grid's triangles: the game's own form at 16 segments per patch edge, where the game has at most 15 and its own triangles. The alternative, the map as a BC5 texture read per pixel, was not taken: between grid points it is another function (the game interpolates z, it does not form it per pixel, and a point-sampled map would step from texel to texel on a cliff), and it needs a new format in the texture cache for a map that is not a whole number of blocks, a fourth table slot and the cache's per-frame content check of an 0.8 MB texture, all for the same values at the grid points. The per-vertex normal is also what the replay tool computes (`SquareGrid` and `grid_interp` in `terrain_replay.py`: the same grid points, linear over the same two triangles per grid quad). The side texture goes through the texture cache like the colour, with its own sampler by its fetch constant and its own scale into the host texture. The numbers of `TerrainNormalFromTexel`, `TerrainSteepSlope`, `TerrainSteepWeights`, `TerrainSideUvs` and `TerrainSteepMix` in `test_terrain_patch.cpp` are those of the replay test's `SteepTest` (flat ground; 35 degrees, s = 0.5311767; vertical facing x; vertical facing y; the threshold), the statements of the HLSL are pinned one by one in `test_native_shaders.cpp`, and `BuildTerrainNormals` is pinned on a synthetic map against the decode's values.
- *The far coordinate.* It is evaluated per vertex when the grid is built, not per pixel: `BuildTerrainFarUvs` (`terrain_patch.h`) reads the two `k_16` tables at the 17 knots of each patch edge with the vertex shader's own addressing (`TerrainFarKnot`) and point sampling, and the geometry cache keeps the result as the draw's UV buffer (`TerrainFarUvs`, key `TerrainFarUvKey` with the content hash of both tables). The native grid has 16 quads per patch edge, so its points are exactly the knots, u depends on the column and v on the row only, and the interpolation between them is the linear run between knots that the replay computes; the game's own vertices read the same tables at the nearest knot. The alternative, two `R16` textures sampled per pixel, would need a new decode in the texture cache, two more table slots and a hand-written knot interpolation in the shader for the same values. `test_terrain_patch.cpp` builds the replay test's two tables and patch and pins the replay's numbers (u 0.375 and v 0.75 at the point the Python test checks). The tables are linear textures: their rows are the fetch pitch as in the SDK's base-level rule, which `DecodeHeightMap` now uses for a linear map (before, a 256-byte row alignment that no heightmap had exercised; the two rules agree for every table of the two session-1 frames, pitches 896, 256 and 128 texels: `task10\census_session1_bridge_f2.txt`, `task10\census_session1_trees_f2.txt`).
- *All or nothing per square.* A square is one patch rectangle of the layer builder: the tile origin `c113.xy`, the sub-patch and the cell size of the record, compared as the rectangle `TerrainPatchRect` computes exactly as the grid's first point. Its underlay is a draw of the adaptive builder with a patch that contains the square's centre. When any draw of a square cannot have the material this frame, the square is one opaque flat clay draw (its first layer, or the underlay itself when that is the draw that failed) and its other layers are left out (`PlanTerrain`, with its cases in `test_clay_logic.cpp`): additive layers are never drawn over flat clay and a square is never partly textured. Every layer draw of the two session-1 frames is one patch (492 and 426 rows); a layer draw of several patches would tie its squares together, so that they fall back together. Reasons, per record: `terrain-inputs` (the inputs are not valid, which for a shader with the steep-ground block includes the block's own: the side texture's or the normal map's fetch constant, `c45` or `c8.x` could not be read; the pixel shader is no terrain shader of the builder; the captured state cannot be drawn as captured, being unread or having a depth function or a blend the RHI cannot express; or the pass has no pipeline for that state because its cache is full or the creation failed: under the default pipeline, opaque with depth less, a layer over its underlay would fail the depth test and a first layer would write its colour unweighted), the texture cache's reason of the colour texture, of the weight atlas or, for a layer with the steep-ground block, of its side texture (`texture-pending`, `format-unsupported`, `texture-dynamic`, `texture-bad`), `uv-unsupported` (the far tables, or the normal map of a layer with the block, could not be read or decoded: `TerrainSteepStatus`; a layer that has the block is drawn with it or its square falls back, the block is never left out), and `terrain-square` for a record that was usable itself: as a status of the flat draw, and as a skip reason of the layers left out. With the texture path off every terrain record is flat clay as before. A flat terrain draw whose depth state cannot be used, or that finds no pipeline of its own, tests less-equal with depth write (`TerrainFlatDefaultKey`, kept as a fifth default pipeline), so that it still covers the underlay at its own depth. A record with more than 4,096 patches (`kMaxTerrainPatches`; the capture admits counts up to 4,194,304), for which no grid is built, has no squares and is not walked by the plan. Since the final fix wave such a record of the adaptive builder makes every square of the frame fall back (it draws nothing and cannot say which squares it lies under: the treatment of an underlay without geometry), and the plan has a work budget (`kTerrainPlanBudget`, 1,048,576 steps: 64 for a layer patch linked to its square, 1 for a square tested against a patch of an underlay without material). A frame over it is not planned by squares: every layer record that has geometry is flat clay and the far draws keep their own state. The bridge frame takes 31,488 steps, and about 160,000 more if none of its 75 far records had its material; 16 garbage layer records of 4,096 patches over an underlay without material took 146 and 218 ms in two runs of a probe before and take 0.0002 ms or less now, and the plan's cost at the budget is 0.5 to 0.9 ms (`finalfix_plan_probe.txt`).
- *Statistics.* A terrain record drawn with the material counts as textured; the `Textures:` line's second share leaves all terrain records out. The log has a line `Terrain: material M of T drawn, flat F, left out S, texture sets N` per window (the distinct sets of colour, weight and side texture bound; "texture pairs" before the steep-ground block), followed by `| without material: <reason> N, ...` when records could not have their material: every such record under its own reason, which the other lines show as `terrain-square` for all but a square's flat draw. A record whose grid or index buffer could not be built is not drawable and makes its square fall back like any other unusable record; since the final fix wave it has its entry after `without material:` too, `no-positions` or `no-indices` by what was missing (before, only the geometry cache counted it, as `skipped_bad_index` or `other` on the log's first clay line; not seen in any run, where both counters read 0). A terrain record that is usable but finds no pipeline for its state (the cache full, or the pipeline not created) falls back under `terrain-inputs` and is counted once in the State line's `(overflow N)`.
- *Not built.* Light, shadow and fog are the lighting sub-project, and with them the normal maps tf2 and tf14 (tf14 is the side projections' own, fetched inside the same block).

**In the captures, and not.** The two captures of 2026-10-06 (`211654`, `211843`), which the counts of this subsection are from, hold: the pairings, counts, order, blend and depth state, bin masks; vertex constants `c0..c3`, `c8`, `c11`, `c46`, `c47`, `c72`, `c113..c115` and fetch constants tf16 to tf19 of every tess row. They do not hold a terrain texture, the pixel shaders' fetch constants, pixel constants at terrain draws, vertex `c4..c6`, `c14`, `c19` or a shader's literal table. The two session-1 captures hold all of these for every tess row (next paragraph): 567 and 449 main-scene tess rows in frame 2, each with `"pconst"`, `"pc"`, `"pbool"`, `"vs_lit"`, `"ps_lit"`, the added vertex constants, `"rs"` with `"2001"`, and the slots its shaders sample (layer rows of `04F2`: tf 0, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 13, 14, 16 to 19; adaptive rows: 3, 4, 5, 13, 16 to 19); no literal table is `null`. Dump sizes as computed: tf16 `0x1B770058` 1,281,024 bytes, tf17 `0x1B8AA071` 802,816, the far tables `0x1B9D6058` / `0x1B9EC058` 85,856 each, the weight atlas tf18 `0x1BA0207A` 1,597,440; `--check-dumps` ends with 0 of 94 and 0 of 36 textures the replay reads short or missing (`session1_bridge_check_dumps_f2_fix2.txt`, `session1_trees_check_dumps_f2_fix2.txt`; the two fog tables tf4 and tf5 have no texture row and the screen-size sun mask tf8 no known size: not read by the unlit replay).

**Discovery rows.** Tess rows now also carry: vertex constants `c4`, `c5`, `c6`, `c14`, `c19` in `"consts"`; `"pconst"` (pixel constants c0..c31, as draw rows); `"pc"` (pixel constants 45, 66, 67, 68, 80, 81, 87, 88, 109, 112 as `{"cN": [4 floats]}`); `"pbool"`; and `"vs_lit"` / `"ps_lit"`, the literal constants of the two shader objects as `[{"reg": first register, "v": [floats]}]` read from the table `GpuLoadShaders` uploads (`xdk_layout.h`, `kShaderLiteralTableField`, `ParseLiteralRuns`; static evidence there; the vertex side was read this way once by the temporary diagnostic of section 12, the pixel side never). All behind the discovery gate, with bounded reads (at most four runs of 64 dwords). With Task 8's change the rows list and dump every texture slot the two shaders sample. `c45` is in `"pc"`, and the pixel literals `c254`, `c255` are whatever the pixel shader's table holds, in `"ps_lit"`. Fix round 1 added two things. `"rs"` of draw and tess rows gains `"2001"`, `RB_COLOR_INFO` (the colour target's format in bits 16 to 19), from device + 0x2884: the second word of the 0x2000 run the builders flush from + 0x2880 (`DeviceRegShadowOffset`, test `test_xdk_layout.cpp`); it is the device shadow only and, unlike the thirteen registers of "Render state", was never compared with the GPU. And texture dumps of `k_16` and `k_DXN` are now the whole base level (`BaseLevelDumpBytes` in `texture_decode.h`, test `test_texture_decode.cpp` with the fetch constants of tf16, tf17, the far tables and tf18): before, every format the renderer does not decode was cut at 65,536 bytes (all 34 and 44 `k_DXN` rows and every other such row of the two captures are exactly that size), which would have left the near heightmap (769 x 769, 1,281,024 bytes) with its first 64 texel rows only, the far tables (85,856 bytes) without their last rows, and the normal map (802,816 bytes) cut too. Other formats the renderer does not decode keep the 64 KB cap. Session 1 ran all of it (previous paragraph). The colour target's format is thereby established: `RB_COLOR_INFO` is `0x00030000` in the adaptive tess rows and 2,834 / 3,286 main-scene draw rows and `0x000C0000` in every layer tess row and 282 / 308 draw rows: format 3, `k_2_10_10_10_FLOAT`, and format 12, `k_2_10_10_10_FLOAT_AS_16_16_16_16` (SDK `xenos.h`). Both are float, so the game's target does not clamp a negative alpha (device shadow only; two values inside one main scene were not expected).

**In the code.** `TerrainMaterialInputs` in `terrain_patch.h` (pure; `MakeTerrainMaterialInputs`, `TerrainUv`, `TerrainLayerWeight`, `TerrainLayerCulled`, `TerrainFade`, `TerrainEyeDistance2`, `TerrainFarKnot`; test `test_terrain_patch.cpp`) holds what the formulas above need from a draw: the fetch constants of the detail or far colour, the base map and the weight cells or the two tables, the three coordinate transforms as functions of the world position, exponent and tint, the weight literals, the fade, the cull reference, the eye and the matrix. For a layer shader with the steep-ground block it also holds the block's inputs (`steep`: the fetch constants of the side texture and of the normal map, `(c8.x, -c8.x)`, `c45.xy` and the two literals; `DecodeNormalMap`, `SampleNormal`, `BuildTerrainNormals` and the block's arithmetic are in the same header). The capture fills it for every terrain record (`FillTerrainMaterial` in `capture.cpp`: the pixel shader must have a terrain entry of the builder's kind; five fetch constants and eleven registers at most; invalid when anything the material reads cannot be read or is not finite, the block's inputs included for a shader that has it, so that such a record's square falls back as a whole; the record grew from 992 to 1,064 bytes) and records the pixel shader and its kind in the record's material. `ps-albedo.json` has the eight shaders as kinds `terrain-layer`, `terrain-far` and `terrain-far-fade` (`2F8A...`) with `base_slot` 3 and `detail_slot` 0 or 13; the generator writes `FABLE2_PS_TERRAIN(hash, kind, base_slot, detail_slot, side_slot, steep_threshold, steep_epsilon)`, which replaces `FABLE2_PS_KIND`. The three entries of the shaders with the steep-ground block have `"steep": {"threshold": 0.99609375, "epsilon": 0.0001, "side_slot": 13}` (the other five end in `-1, 0.0f, 0.0f`): the two numbers are literals of the shader, zero in the device bank, so they come from the table and not from guest memory, entered from the shaders' own literal tables as session 1 captured them ("Constants", last row). The terrain entries have no albedo line, so the albedo lookup does not know the shaders, and a terrain record keeps the capture status `terrain`. From Task 10 on the clay pass draws the record from these inputs ("Native terrain material"). The renderer does not read the base map's part of them (`base_fetch`, `base_uv`, `base_pow`, `base_tint`: the unlit rule) and binds no texture for `base_slot`; the table schema keeps the field for the lighting sub-project. The capture keeps that part apart: the fetch constant of `base_slot`, vertex `c14` and pixel `c21` are read into the inputs when they can be, `base_valid` says whether they were, and they decide nothing else: a draw without a texture in that slot, or with a `c21` that is not finite, keeps its material (fix round 1 of Task 10; in commit `086d3cb` either made the inputs invalid as a whole). Every terrain row of the captures has all three.

**Tools.** `tools\xdk_sigmatch\terrain_replay.py` (tests `tests/test_terrain_replay.py`, hand-computed pixels of a two-layer patch, a culled layer, a far patch and a capture written as discovery writes it): `--list` prints a frame's squares with their layer draws, `--patch K --out picture.png` replays one square (the underlay if a colour draw of the adaptive builder covers it, then the layers with their captured blend), `--weights picture.png` writes the layer weights of all squares as a map. The replay evaluates the steep-ground block for the layers whose shader has it (Task 10b; it printed "not evaluated" before): the normal map through `decode_dxn`, the normal and the height at the square's 17 x 17 grid points as the vertex shader reads them and linear over the clay pass's two triangles per grid quad in between (`SquareGrid`, `grid_interp`), the slope, the weights and the two side fetches (`steep_inputs`, `steep_mix`); the two literals are read from the row's `"ps_lit"` in the register each shader keeps them in (`STEEP_LITERALS`) and refused when they do not look like the block's. `--no-steep` leaves the block out and gives the earlier output; a capture that lacks an input of the block is refused by name with that hint. `--steep-share` prints the share of grid points with s below the threshold and with s = 0 (item 1). Further options: `--weight-sums` (how the clamped layer weights add up at the 17 x 17 grid points of every square, apart for squares whose first layer replaces and squares over the underlay), `--patch K --locate` (the square's corners projected with the row's `c0..c3` into a 1120 x 720 frame, at the ground's height; since the final fix wave a missing or cut heightmap is refused by name here too, where the corners used to be placed at height 0 with a note), `--check-dumps` (every texture the frame's tess rows name, with its dump's size against its base level's); `--weights` also counts the cull edges above (since the final fix wave over the weight blocks of the layers that can be culled only: a square's first layer, with `c114.x` = -1, is never culled and is left out; session 1's count of 0 pairs was taken over all layers and includes them). A texture's dump is found by fetch dwords 1 and 2 (base address, format, endian and size; no such pair occurs with two pitch or tiling fields in either capture, while the sampler bits of the other dwords vary: by all six dwords 138 of 171 and 163 of 187 distinct fetch constants of the rows had a dump, by the two 141 and 165). Nothing is read as zero: a dump shorter than its base level, a texture format the tool does not decode and a missing input are each refused by name, with exit code 2 (the first version padded short dumps with zeros, which with the 64 KB cut would have given every square the height 0 and a wrong fade). It takes everything from a capture's tess and texture rows, and refuses a capture that lacks an input by naming it: on the bridge capture `--list` gives 92 squares (`replay_list_bridge_211654_f2.txt`) and `--patch 0` answers `the tess row has no vertex constant c14, no "pconst" (pixel constants c0..c31): the capture was taken before terrain rows recorded them`. The numbers the tool and `MakeTerrainMaterialInputs` make from one set of constants are pinned as the same literals in both test files (the three transforms, weight, cull, fade, distance, table knots). `texture_thumb.py --block-stat` prints, for a `k_DXT3A` texture, the mean step between neighbouring texels inside a 4 x 4 block and across a block edge, both ways (`block_step_stats`): on a smooth synthetic image the correct layout gives 5.99 / 5.72 horizontally and 7.74 / 6.49 vertically; mirrored nibbles leave the inside value and give 40.5 across; exchanged nibble pairs give 10.0 / 17.9; mirrored rows 52.6 across vertically; exchanged row pairs 13.1 / 23.3. So a wrong order shows as a ratio far from 1 on its own axis, in the across value always and in the inside value for the exchanged pairs.

**Run, and what remains.** Every run below is a gated driver run that ended `RUN CLEAN` with exit code 0 and live `[frame] guest` lines; evidence is in `out\native-evidence\materials\`.

*Game session 1* (2026-10-07, commit `f662dfa`; `session1_*`; runs `fable_2_257.log` at the bridge and `fable_2_258.log` by the trees, with the plans of "Alpha and missing shaders") checked the reading on real data, with the native ground still flat clay: the row keys and dump sizes ("In the captures, and not"); the weight atlas's decode (Task 8's real-dump test ran and passed on `1BA02000_7432BE67B3E334CA.bin`, 1,597,440 bytes; `--block-stat` gives in-block and across-block steps of 13.08 / 12.77 horizontally and 13.33 / 12.99 vertically, ratios 0.98 and 0.97, so nibble and row order are right; the 1:1 crops show a grid of 33-texel cells with single-texel edges); the weight sums and cull edges ("The weight cells"); the constants and literals ("Constants"); tf3 and the tint; the weight map of the bridge (`session1_bridge_weights_f2_x5.png`: one coherent map whose bands and blobs continue across the squares, no uncovered pixel inside the ground); and the replay of six squares around the eye (`session1_bridge_mosaic_sheet_default_notint_nobase.png`): a tan dirt patch ringed by moss and grass with a group of rocks, each material in its own texture at one repeat per 8 units, joined without seams. `--locate` puts that dirt patch at the left of the bridge, at the edge between squares 30 / 31 and 48 / 49, where the emulated frame has a dirt patch surrounded by grass.

*Task 10* (2026-10-07; `task10_*`, scripts in `task10\`) ran the native material:

| Run | Plan (after `23:E,27:S,30:E,35:E`) | Log | Terrain records with the material | Textured of drawn | fps in live windows |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bridge | `56:VIEW=off,62:VIEW=native,68:VIEW=split,74:VIEW=overlay,80:VIEW=native,84:DISC=8/1` | `task10_bridge1_fable_2_263.log` | 564 of 564, 77 texture pairs | 907 to 911 of 919 to 923 (98%; 96% of the non-terrain draws) | 30.0 |
| trees | `55:LS=0/100/3000,59:RS=-60/0/1500,62:LS=0/100/2500,69:RS=-60/0/600,72:RS=60/0/600,76:VIEW=off,82:VIEW=native,88:VIEW=split,94:VIEW=overlay,100:VIEW=native,104:DISC=8/1,108:VIEW=off,112:VIEW=native` | `task10_trees1_fable_2_264.log` | 446 of 446, 25 texture pairs | 846 to 862 of 850 to 866 (99%; 99%) | 30.0 (29.2 in the window of the walk) |
| third spot: the hero walks 3.5 s toward the camera, which then stands farther back on the bridge. It is the same bridge seen from its other side, not another place: the three spots are one small area | `55:LS=0/-100/3500,62:VIEW=off,68:VIEW=native,74:VIEW=split,80:VIEW=overlay,86:VIEW=native` | `task10_back1_fable_2_265.log` | 556 of 556, 79 texture pairs | 965 to 967 of 977 to 979 (98%; 97%) | 29.1 to 29.3 (below) |

No window of any run has a terrain record that is flat or left out, and no reason of the terrain material appears: what stays untextured is `blend-unknown` (27 and 19: `0x014F...`, `0xA17D...`, one `0x401A...`), `no-albedo` and `alpha-unknown` (12 and 3 each: `0xF6D9...`), all from "Alpha and missing shaders". Before the task the same windows read 343 to 347 of 919 to 923 (37%) and 413 to 419 of 863 to 869 (47 to 48%).

What the shots show (emulated, native, split and overlay of one camera position per run, and crops `task10_*_crop_*`, all viewed):

- *Bridge.* The native ground is textured everywhere the emulated ground shows. The dirt patch left of the bridge is tan dirt with the same outline, with grass and plants around it as in the emulated frame (`task10_bridge1_crop_dirt_patch_left_x3_emulated_native.png`). Its mean colour has hue 39.0 degrees and saturation 0.46 against 38.0 and 0.47 for the dirt of the replay mosaic's detail-colour panel, at 0.875 of its brightness (the native pass multiplies by its facet shade, 0.75 to 1; that the whole difference is the shade is inference) (`task10\hues.py`, `task10_bridge1_hues.txt`; the rectangles were picked by eye); the emulated dirt has hue 43 degrees and is about 1.45 times brighter, being lit. Across the lake the hills are rock with moss and grass on the flatter parts, the island has grass and a dirt top, and the slope at the right has grass with the tan path running up it where the emulated frame has it. No seam shows between squares, no square stands out brighter or darker, and no band shows where the layers give way to the far colour; the far ground is point sampled, as its fetch constants say, and looks blockier close up.
- *Trees.* Most of the ground is under ferns. Where it shows it agrees: the tan path curving behind the fence at the left with moss beside it, the yellow-green moss behind the posts at the right, dark earth between the ferns at the bottom (`task10_trees1_crop_path_left_x3_emulated_native.png`, `..._ground_behind_posts_x2_...`, `..._ground_between_ferns_x2_...`).
- *Third spot.* The dirt path at the lower right is tan dirt with its grass edge where the emulated frame has it; across the water the shore has grass slopes, a pale strip along the waterline and a path, and the island its grass and dirt, all as in the emulated frame (`task10_back1_crop_dirt_path_right_x3_emulated_native.png`, `task10_back1_crop_shore_left_x2_emulated_native.png`). The two large far mountains are untextured meshes.
- *Different from the emulated frame, as expected.* The ground is darker and has no light, shadow or fog; steep ground shows the flat ground's texture stretched into streaks where the emulated frame shows rock (the bank under the bridge at the lower left, the near slope at the right: `task10_bridge1_crop_near_ground_bottom_left_x3_emulated_native.png`, `task10_bridge1_crop_grass_right_x3_emulated_native.png`): the steep-ground block. Water is flat clay and the farthest mountain, a mesh, is untextured.
- *The depth pre-pass draws* of "Pre-pass draws and the depth range" (3 per frame, skipped as `depth-only`) leave no gap: the squares they cover get their colour from the layers.

Cost against the runs of "Alpha and missing shaders" (Task 7d: `task7d_bridge_fable_2_262.log`, `task7d_trees_fable_2_261.log`; bridge, then trees): guest work median 7.5 to 7.7 ms (7.4 to 7.6) and 6.5 to 6.8 ms (6.7 to 6.8); capture median with records on 1.31 to 1.34 ms (1.30) and 1.33 to 1.35 ms (1.35 to 1.40), with the view off 0.047 and 0.041 ms; clay `hash` 0.49 to 0.60 ms (0.50 to 0.54) and 0.45 to 0.57, `decode` 0.11 to 0.19 (0.10 to 0.24) and 0.68 to 0.83 (0.67 to 0.78), `record` 0.30 to 0.44 (0.21 to 0.30) and 0.27 to 0.42, `max total` 2.7 to 4.5 ms (2.0 to 4.5) and 3.3 to 4.1 ms in the four windows after the walk (3.0 to 3.2, and 11.3 in one), 15.0 and 12.2 ms in the two windows of the walk (12.8 and 12.3); the texture line's `decode` 1.09 to 1.16 ms (0.46 to 0.51) and 0.78 to 0.91 ms (0.51 to 0.66), which is the per-frame content check of 115 and 140 more textures; textures resident 188, 26.9 MB (73, 18.2 MB) and 240, 32.5 MB (98 to 100, 20.6 to 22.6 MB); geometry resident 28.0 MB (24.6) and 52.9 MB (45.7 to 45.9), the far coordinates among it; pipelines 13 (8) and 15 (10): two more defaults and three terrain states.

Fix round 1 changed the per-frame content check to mix eight bytes at a time (`SampleHash` in `texture_residency.h`, the same sampled bytes; 2.90 against 0.55 microseconds per call, `task10\fix1_samplehash_bench.txt`). The texture line's `decode` then reads 0.52 and 0.58 ms at the bridge with the same 188 textures in the two bridge windows of run 268, and 0.31 and 0.54 ms in those of run 269 (1.09 to 1.16 before, 0.46 to 0.51 with 73 textures before the task) and 0.38 to 0.58 ms at the third spot with 212 (1.19 to 1.20 before): `task10_fix1_ab1_windows.txt`, `task10_fix1_ab2_windows.txt`. The trees were not run again.

The third spot draws more (1,003 to 1,005 drawable records) and is the one place where native-view windows have read below 30.0 fps. Before fix round 1: 29.1 to 29.3 in the three windows of the run in the table, 29.8 and 30.0 in a second run of the same walk (`task10_back2_ab_fable_2_266.log`), 30.0 with the view off, and 30.0 with the texture path off (`--fable2_native_textures=false`, `task10_back3_flat_fable_2_267.log`). Guest work and clay CPU were the same in the slow and the fast run, so those runs did not establish a cause. After fix round 1, two runs of the walk that alternate 30 s with the view off and 30 s of native view twice (`...,55:LS=0/-100/3500,62:VIEW=off,92:VIEW=native,122:VIEW=off,152:VIEW=native`, 186 s; `task10_fix1_ab1_fable_2_268.log`, `task10_fix1_ab2_fable_2_269.log`; tables `task10_fix1_ab*_windows.txt`) give, per whole 300-frame window:

| Run | View off (4 windows each) | Native view (5 windows each) | Guest work median, off / native | Clay hash / decode / record, max total | Texture line decode |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 30.0, 30.0, 30.0, 30.0 | 29.9, 29.4, 30.0, 30.0, 30.0 | 6.3 to 6.5 / 8.0 to 8.2 ms (p90 8.4 to 8.9) | 0.59 to 0.68 / 0.54 to 0.78 / 0.33 to 0.49, 2.8 to 4.0 ms (8.9 in the first window after a switch) | 0.43 to 0.58 ms |
| 2 | 30.0, 30.0, 30.0, 30.0 | 30.0, 30.0, 30.0, 30.0, 30.0 | 6.3 to 6.5 / 8.0 to 8.1 ms (p90 8.4 to 8.6) | 0.47 to 0.65 / 0.51 to 0.72 / 0.29 to 0.45, 2.8 to 2.9 ms (8.3 in the first window after a switch) | 0.38 to 0.57 ms |

So with the view off every window reads 30.0, and with the native view eight of ten read 30.0, one 29.9 and one 29.4 (run 1, 105 to 116 s; that window has the run's highest clay `decode`, 0.78 ms, and the highest `max total` outside a switch, 4.0 ms, against 0.54 to 0.58 and 2.8 to 3.1 in the others). In these two runs the native view does not stay below 30.0 at this spot; a single window can dip, which no window with the view off did. Whether the faster content check is why the three slow windows of the first run did not recur is not established: one of the two earlier runs was not slow either. The dips are read as the native pass's load on the GPU thread and the GPU on top of the emulated frame at the heaviest spot visited (inference), and are recorded as a limitation of the debug view (controller ruling: not tuned further). A third run was disturbed 0.4 s before its end (`RUN DISTURBED: input at 185.6 s`, the user came back) and is not used. In all of these runs every terrain record has its material (556 of 556, 79 texture pairs), and the native shot at 170 s of run 1 equals the earlier shot of the spot outside the hero and the dog (0.00% of the pixels differ by more than 8 levels).

*Task 10b* (2026-10-07; `task10b*`, scripts in `task10b\`) built the steep-ground block ("Native terrain material", "Steep ground") and checked it without the game: the unit tests of the capture's inputs, the decode and the block's arithmetic with the replay test's numbers; the decode on the real normal map ("The normal map"); the real steep share (item 1); and the replay of three steep squares of the bridge frame without and with the block (squares 80, 51 and 29 of frame 2 of `task10_bridge1_capture`; `task10b\replay_bridge_cliff_squares_x4.png`, viewed: without the block the squares are grass and moss with some rock, with it their steep parts are grey-brown rock and the grass keeps to the flat spots; the replay looks straight down, so a side-projected rock face is seen foreshortened). **In the game** the block was first seen in the user-present runs of plan Task 11 (2026-10-07, 12:32 to 12:44): `RUN UNCHECKED` runs started with `-UserPresent` under the user's authorisation, after the gated driver had refused five launches because the user was at the machine (`task10b_bridge1_refused1_driver_output.txt`, `..._refused2_...`, `task11_bridge1_refused1_driver_output.txt` to `..._refused3_...`); they are not gated runs. "Validation" below has the runs, the checks made on them, the crops and the numbers. In short: the steep faces in view are grey-brown rock where Task 10's shots had the flat texture stretched into streaks (the rock under the right rail and the bank under the bridge at the bridge, the rock face behind the fence at the left by the trees, a strip under the bridge at the third spot), with the emulated outline where the emulated frame is clear, without streaks, without a hard line where the block switches on and without a step at a square's edge in those crops; flat ground is unchanged against Task 10's shots (the dirt patch differs in 0.09% of its pixels by more than 24 levels, the far hills in 0.00%); every terrain record has its material (564 of 564, 446 of 446, 556 of 556; flat 0, left out 0); pipelines are 13, 15 and 13 as before with `invalid 0`; the frame has 86, 33 and 89 texture sets against 77, 25 and 79 pairs; at the bridge 3 more textures (0.3 MB) and 0.2 to 0.3 MB more geometry are resident; every window without a capture reads 30.0 fps. The hills across the lake did not change: they are drawn from the far colour texture, which already held their rock.

Remains:

1. The steep-ground block was seen in the game in four small areas only, standing still, in user-present runs ("Validation"): a long cliff, a cliff seen from above and the ground while moving are user check 23; gated runs of the last commit and a split and an overlay shot of the third spot are under "Still missing" there.
2. The fallback of a square has not been seen on screen: in every logged window all terrain records had their material. It is covered by the unit tests of `PlanTerrain` and `TerrainMaterialStatus` only; the frames in which textures are still pending fall into the fade-in at about 41 s, before any shot (bridge run: two frames with 118 and 1 drawn records pending, `[native] textures: burst` lines; that terrain records were among them is inference), and the run went on without an error.
3. Other regions (town, Bower Lake, interiors) and the ground while moving: user check 23. A region whose terrain uses a pixel shader outside the eight of the table shows flat ground there with the reason `terrain-inputs`.
4. A pixel-for-pixel comparison with the emulated ground is not possible while that is lit; the comparison above is of place, outline and hue.
5. Single native-view windows below 30.0 fps at the third spot (above): a recorded limitation of the debug view.
6. From session 1, still open: the sun and light constants read zero in the tess rows; the two fog tables have no texture row and the sun mask no known dump size (not read by the unlit rule).

### Validation (plan Task 11)

Written 2026-10-07 for commit `4d74e0e` (the steep-ground block, plan Task 10b; the commits after it change documents only). The release binary is the one built at 10:47 that day from `4d74e0e` (`fable_2.exe`, 109,342,208 bytes; `task11\build_step0.txt`; native suite rc 0 with 25 `PASS:` lines, Python `Ran 368 tests ... OK (skipped=2)`), and it is the binary of every run of 2026-10-07 from 12:29 on.

**How the last commit was run.** The idle gate refused every gated launch for `4d74e0e`, the user being at the machine: two in Task 10b and three in Task 11 (10:51, 11:22, 11:52; `task11_bridge1_refused1_driver_output.txt` to `..._refused3_...`, each `REFUSED: user present or state unknown (input, audio) after waiting up to 1800 s. ... Nothing launched.`, exit code 10). The user then authorised runs while at the machine ("run it anyways. i'm fine with it"), and the controller started six runs in the user's session between 12:29 and 12:44 with `tools\drive_game.ps1 -UserPresent`. **These are not gated runs.** Their verdict line is `RUN UNCHECKED: -UserPresent given, no idle gate and no watch` (exit code 0), `drive_game_runs.log` reads `gate=USER PRESENT OVERRIDE (gate and watch skipped)` for each, and nothing watched for input during them. They are called "user-present runs" below. Because nothing guaranteed an undisturbed machine, each was checked window by window for the freeze signature and for input before anything of it was used:

- *Was the camera where the plans put it?* Yes, as far as it can be checked. The eye (`c19.xyz` of every tess row, frame 2 of the two captures; `task11\eye_rows_bridge_up2_f2.txt`, `eye_rows_trees_up1_f2.txt`) is (82.07, 157.34, 50.17) at the bridge and (84.46, 171.93, 49.76) by the trees, the values of every earlier capture, with the same rows per terrain shader (bridge 230 / 118 / 92 / 31 / 18 / 3 layer rows and 57 / 18 adaptive ones; trees 293 / 74 / 23 / 13 / 18 / 5 and 11 / 12). The third spot has no capture; its drawn counts (977 to 979 of 1,003 to 1,005) are those of the gated runs 268 and 269, and its native shot differs from Task 10's only on the hero, the dog and three small strips of ground (below). So the `F6` presses changed the view and nothing else that shows. No shot has another window over the game.
- *The window was larger* than in the gated runs: shots of 1942 x 1136 with the picture 1920 x 1080 at (11, 45), against 1296 x 759 with 1280 x 720 at (8, 31), exactly 1.5 times. Crops were taken at the same relative rectangle and the larger picture resized to the smaller one (Lanczos) before any pixel comparison (`task11\shots.py`, `task11\diffmap.py`); the resize alone changes pixels by more than 8 levels in regions that did not change: 0.94% of them in the far hills, 1.73% in the dirt patch and 1.59% between the ferns; above 24 levels it changes 0.00% to 0.09%, which is why changes are counted above 24 levels.
- *The machine was in use.* Guest work medians are about 1 ms above the gated runs in every view (renderer off 7.0 to 7.4 ms against 6.3 ms in section 12), so costs are compared inside this session, not across sessions.
- *The menu steps of the plans are at fixed times.* The plans press A at 23.0 s. The game's loading phase (the first 600 frames, work median 39 ms with no swap wait; its end is the log's second `[frame] guest` line) ended at 22.74 to 22.91 s in the gated runs, so there the press comes 0.09 to 0.26 s after it. In `bridge_up1` it ended at 24.70 s, 1.7 s after the press: the press was lost, the remaining presses chose "New Game", and the run stood at the dialog "You have already reached the maximum number of save games ..." until it was closed (`task11_bridge_up1_STUCK_shot_60s.png`, viewed). Four of the other five runs (`bridge_up2`, `trees_up1`, `third_up1`, `third_alt_up1`) then used the same plans with every time 6 s later; their loading phase ended at 22.99 to 23.50 s, more than 5 s before the press. The fifth, `off_up1`, was started with the default plan, not shifted: its loading phase ended at 23.02 s, 0.02 s after the press at 23.00 s, and that press was taken. So a press 0.02 s before the end of the loading phase got through and one 1.7 s before it was lost; where between the two the limit lies is not known (`finalfix_menu_timing.txt`: the times of the second `[frame] guest` line and of the first press in the twenty logs, from their time stamps). A fixed-time plan is a limitation: on a slower start it runs into the wrong menu. Since the final fix wave `tools\drive_game.ps1 -PlanShift N` moves a plan, its screenshot times and its total N seconds later (0 to 30); the driver does not detect the menu. A start with `-BootSave HeroNNN` (the user's boot-save branch, merged into this one before the final check; `docs\boot-save.md`) has no menu steps: the game loads the named save itself and the default key plan is dropped, so no press can come too early. The limitation remains for plans that press through the menu.

- *What the block costs*, read at the bridge, the one place where the run had the native view on from the start as the gated run had: 9 more texture sets per frame (a detail texture counts once per side texture it is drawn with), 3 more textures and 0.3 MB resident (the rock-face textures), 0.2 to 0.3 MB more geometry (the normals), no more pipelines, no measurable change of the capture median. By the trees and at the third spot fewer textures and less geometry are resident than in the gated runs: resident is cumulative per run, and in these two runs the view was off, or being cycled, for part of the time the world loaded in (the `F6` presses), so the textures that only the draws of those seconds use were not asked for (an inference from the totals, 188 against 240 and 159 against 212 textures; which textures differ was not listed); the sets rose by 8 and 10.
- *The native view against view off, inside one run* (`third_alt_up1`): guest work median 9.1 to 9.2 ms in the four whole native-view windows against 7.3 to 7.8 ms in the four whole view-off windows, a difference of about 1.6 ms, as in the gated runs (8.0 to 8.2 against 6.3 to 6.5). At the bridge and by the trees the windows hold the six-second view tour, so their work medians are of mixed views; the one whole native-view window at the bridge without a capture reads 8.7 ms [7.6]. The renderer off at the bridge reads 7.0 to 7.4 ms (`off_up1`) against 8.3 to 8.7 ms in native view there in `bridge_up2`.
- *Not to be held against the `-BootSave` runs of the final check* (runs 277 to 279, "Final fix wave" below). There the guest work median is 10.7 to 11.7 ms at the bridge, 9.6 to 10.6 ms by the trees and 9.9 to 10.1 ms with the renderer off: about 2 to 3 ms above the figures of this table with the renderer on and with it off, so the rise is not the native pass. The capture median with records on reads 1.47 to 1.62 and 1.41 to 1.64 ms there. Whether the machine's load or the game's background loading after a boot-save start is behind it was not separated.
- *In the logs and not from this sub-project's last commit:* by the trees one logged frame of the walk (frame 1800) skips two draws as `bad-index`, vertex shader `0x5DF7A6CDFB278E98`, and has four `ps-unknown` draws. The same shader was `bad-index` in Task 1's walking run (`task1_autoplay_stick_run.log`, frames 1800 and 2100); the gated trees runs logged other moments of the walk and do not have it. It is a draw on the way from the bridge to the fence whose index buffer the capture rejects; not looked into.
- Startup: no `shader compile failed`, `pixel shader compile failed`, `pipeline creation failed`, `binding layout creation failed`, `heap exhausted` or `texture path off` line in any of the four native runs; `skipped_bad_index 0 other 0` on every clay line.

**Steep ground against the emulated frame and against Task 10** (plan Task 10b's game check; emulated | Task 10's native shot | native now, the same rectangle; all viewed):

- *Bridge, the rock under the right rail* (`task11_bridge_up2_crop_grass_right_x2_emulated_task10native_native.png`, `task11_bridge_up2_crop_rock_right_1to1_x2_emulated_native.png`). Emulated: a rounded grey rock face below the grass slope. Task 10: the same area in green, the grass texture pulled into blurred diagonal streaks. Now: a grey-brown mottled rock face with the emulated outline, grass above it, and a soft change from grass to rock along the top; the rock's pattern is not stretched. It is browner and flatter than the emulated rock, which is lit and has relief. 11.1% of the rectangle's pixels changed by more than 24 levels.
- *Bridge, the bank under the bridge at the lower left* (`..._crop_near_ground_bottom_left_x3_...`, `..._crop_bank_bottom_left_1to1_x2_emulated_native.png`). Task 10: bright green streaks between the timbers. Now: grey mottled rock in the same gaps (21.3% of the pixels changed). The emulated frame is in deep shadow there and half under the HUD's button wheel: it shows a dark, mottled bank with ochre patches, no grass; that it is this rock texture cannot be read from it.
- *Trees, the rock face behind the fence at the left* (`task11_trees_up1_crop_left_edge_x3_emulated_task10native_native.png`). Emulated: a blue-grey rock face beside a mossy boulder. Task 10: a brown-green smear. Now: a grey rock face in that place, the boulder unchanged (5.2% of the pixels changed).
- *Third spot* (`task11_third_alt_up1_crop_bottom_right_x3_...`): a strip seen between the beams at the bottom right was stretched green and is rock now; the emulated frame is dark there. The run has an emulated and a native shot of this spot only; **no split or overlay shot of the third spot exists at this commit** (the run that had them froze).
- *What did not change:* everything else. The hills across the lake look as in Task 10 (rock with moss and grass on the flatter parts): they are the far colour texture, which already held their rock, not layers with the block (`..._crop_far_hills_x1_...`, `task11_third_alt_up1_crop_shore_left_x1_...`). The dirt patch, the grass slopes, the paths and the ground under the ferns are unchanged (`task11_bridge_up2_diffmap_task10native_native.png`, `task11_trees_up1_diffmap_...`, `task11_third_alt_up1_diffmap_...`: the white pixels are the steep places and the hero and the dog in another pose).
- *Not judged:* a seam between a square with the block and its neighbour could not be looked for along any length (the steep areas in view are 100 to 250 pixels across); the faces are seen from one side, standing still; nothing was replayed against the offline tool for these squares.

**What the pictures show, emulated against native** (`task11_bridge_up2_shot_66s.png` emulated, `_72s` native, `_78s` split, `_84s` overlay; `task11_trees_up1_shot_86s.png`, `_92s`, `_98s`, `_104s`; `task11_third_alt_up1_shot_86s.png` emulated, `_176s` native; all viewed):

- *Ground.* Emulated: lit grass, dirt, moss and rock with shadows and haze. Native: the same materials in the same places, darker and flat, without shadow, haze or fog; rock on the steep faces (above). In the split shots the ground continues across the seam and in the overlay shots the rock under the right rail and the paths lie on the emulated ones without a second outline. The lake is flat pale clay. The sky is black. The large far mountains are meshes without albedo: dark at the bridge, plain pale clay at the third spot, where they stand out.
- *The far side of the lake* is where the two pictures differ most: the emulated frame shows wooded hills in haze, the native frame bare rock-and-moss hills with sharp edges, because the distant trees are not drawn and nothing fades with distance.
- *Grass, ferns, flowers.* The same silhouettes in the same places, standing still, unlit; no card is a rectangle. At this window size the native ferns by the trees look duller, thicker and less crisp than the emulated ones: the clay target is 1120 x 720 stretched to the window, every frond is one flat colour, and fading cards are dot grids.
- *Leaves.* Closed crowns of one flat mid-green on the same boughs, fully opaque; the emulated crowns have light, shade and gaps of sky.
- *Trunks, fence posts, the hero, the crows.* Textured and in place; the split seam runs through the hero without a step.
- *The dog.* Its dark textured body with white paws, without fur (the fur shells are left out as `blend-unknown`); the emulated dog is brown and furry.

**Known limitations.**

1. **No lighting.** Everything is unlit: darker and flatter than the emulated frame, with no shadow, fog or haze. For the ground the baked light map tf3 and its tint `c21` are dropped by ruling, and with them the normal maps tf2 and tf14, so rock faces have no relief; leaves are one flat green. A number-for-number comparison with the emulated frame is not possible; the comparisons above are of place, outline and hue.
2. **The last commit was seen only in runs made with the user present** (`RUN UNCHECKED`), on a machine in use, with key presses reaching the game in two of them. Counts, pictures and frame rate agree with the gated runs of the commits before; absolute times are about 1 ms higher and not comparable across sessions. Of the six runs one never left the menu and one froze.
3. **Steep ground was seen in four small areas**, from one side, standing still (above). A long cliff, a cliff seen from above and the ground while moving are user check 23.
4. **Wind sway and the push away from characters are not drawn** (section 12). This is what put the yellow flower in front of a fern frond in game session 1 ("Plan Task 7c"); the sway itself was not replayed.
5. **Fading draws are dot grids.** Alpha-to-mask is applied at one sample per pixel where the game has two: a draw the game shows half transparent is solid, and below that a grid of one, two or three pixels in four, more conspicuous than the game's fade; below an alpha of an eighth it is missing.
6. **Draws left out as `blend-unknown`**, per frame: the fur shells `0x014F8A02DB7B19CA` (15; no colour texture readable), `0xA17D8AEC3A817D45` (12 at the bridge, 3 by the trees; a mask-texture discard without an albedo), one draw of `0x401A01FD4E5F8757` by the trees, and the rows of `0x44B567635EB10620` that blend with a constant colour (16 of its 20 rows), which the RHI cannot express. Any other blended draw whose colour is unknown is left out the same way.
7. **`alpha-unknown`**: `0xF6D98C7B4D98438B` (12 / 3 / 11 draws; it has no albedo either), drawn as flat clay without its test. The instanced plants' distance dissolve is left out, so a field of plants ends in a hard line where the game thins it.
8. **Water is flat clay; particles, billboards, the sky and the distant trees are not drawn** (30 `unsupported-prim` draws and the `no-transform` billboards of section 12).
9. **The far ground is point sampled**, as its fetch constants say, and looks blocky close up.
10. **Blended draws are not sorted**; they are drawn in capture order.
11. **Entries that rest on dump traces without a screenshot**: `0x44B567635EB10620` (its object was behind ferns), `0x648B965C200A5113` (in no shot), `0x14217F5C1118D00F` and the ivy draw of `0x00E0...` (not told apart on screen), `0x09CB69BD265B58D1` (one draw 33 units away, not identified). `"divide_by_alpha": false` for `0x8D90...` and `0x648B...` rests on the bool word (`b128`) and is undecided on screen. The colour division of families A and B was not compared with and without.
12. **Device shadow only**: the sample count (`RB_SURFACE_INFO`), the colour target's format (`RB_COLOR_INFO`) and the bool constant word were not compared with the GPU, unlike the thirteen registers of "Render state". Stencil state and the blend constant are not captured.
13. **Terrain was seen in one region only**, at three spots around one bridge. Another region may use terrain pixel shaders outside the table's eight: its ground is then flat clay with the reason `terrain-inputs` (user checks 23 and 26). `DecodeNormalMap` accepts one layout of the normal map; another gives `uv-unsupported`.
14. **The terrain fallback has never been seen on screen**: no logged window of any run had a terrain record without its material. It is covered by the unit tests of `PlanTerrain`, `TerrainMaterialStatus` and `TerrainSteepStatus`; the glue in `clay_pass.cpp` has run only on the path where nothing falls back.
15. **Terrain approximations**: layers were added in display space until the lighting sub-project (transition bands about one weight texel wide were slightly darker than the game's; section 14, "Linear target"); a negative weight is clamped; the grid has 16 segments per patch edge where the game tessellates adaptively; weight and normal are the game's vertex values interpolated over that grid; the normal map's values are not rounded to 8 bits (a difference of up to about 0.05 in the slope value s, inside the band of 30 to 40 degrees where s runs from 1 to 0).
16. **The destination alpha of the clay target is not meaningful** once terrain layers are drawn: they write their blend alpha into it. Nothing reads it today (the composite takes the colour only).
17. **Single native-view windows below 30.0 fps at the third spot** in the gated runs at `6a027bc` (one of ten at 29.4, one at 29.9, none with the view off), read as the native pass's load on top of the emulated GPU; not tuned. The user-present run of `4d74e0e` read 30.0 in its four whole native-view windows and in the five windows that hold a view switch or the walk; that is one run on another day's machine load, not the same base as the ten gated windows.
18. **Geometry and textures resident differ between runs at the same draws**: resident is cumulative, so it follows which keys the run touched while the world loaded in (24.6 against 22.3 MB of geometry at the bridge in two gated runs; 159 against 212 textures at the third spot when the view was off during the load).
19. **Autoplay plans are fixed in time.** In the gated runs the first menu press comes 0.09 to 0.26 s after the end of the loading phase; a press 0.02 s before that end was still taken (`off_up1`), and one 1.7 s before it was lost and sent the plan into "New Game" (`bridge_up1`). Where the limit lies in between is not known. Four of the five other runs of this validation used the plans 6 s later; the driver's `-PlanShift` does that for any plan (final fix wave), and it does not detect the menu. A `-BootSave` start has no menu steps (`docs\boot-save.md`; the final check used one): the limitation remains for plans that press through the menu.
20. **Three spots of one region, standing still.** Town, a field, an interior and the camera moving are user checks.

**Still missing.**

A split and an overlay shot of the third spot at `4d74e0e`, and its three windows after the six-second view tour (the run froze): `.\tools\drive_game.ps1 -Total 106 -Shots "72,78,84,90,101" -Plan "29:E,33:S,36:E,41:E,61:LS=0/-100/3500,68:VIEW=off,74:VIEW=native,80:VIEW=split,86:VIEW=overlay,92:VIEW=native" -GameArgs "--fable2_native_render=true","--fable2_native_view=native"`. The terrain inputs are now a side array of the frame (`FrameScene::terrain`, read through `TerrainOf`), the record is 616 bytes, and a frame's buffers are reused by the frames after it. Whether that wins back the capture time the sub-project added with records on (expected: about 0.1 to 0.25 ms per frame) has not been measured in the game yet.
    - `PlanTerrain`'s table of squares allocates a node per square per frame (about 100 at the bridge).
    - The weight atlas is content-checked sparsely like every texture; a partial update between the sampled bytes would be missed (a full hash of that one texture would close it; it is static in the captures).
    - When the RHI's texture-table heap is exhausted the pass keeps the previous texture set for the draw.
    - The clay shaders' behaviour is pinned by their text, their compiled constant layouts and pure C++ mirrors, not by rendered pixels.
    - `bad-index` 2 for vertex shader `0x5DF7A6CDFB278E98` on the walk to the trees (item 10; older than this sub-project).
    - The plans' menu steps are at fixed times (item 9; `-PlanShift` moves them, nothing waits for the menu). A `-BootSave` start has no menu steps.
    - `PlanTerrain`'s last loop (a layer record with a bad square makes all its squares bad, repeated until nothing changes) is outside the work budget: 8,191 layer records of two patches each, in an order in which each pass carries the fallback one record further, are inside the budget (1,048,448 of 1,048,576 steps) and took 90 to 142 ms in two runs of five repeats (`finalfix_plan_chain_probe.txt`, `-O2`; 0.5 to 1.7 ms with the bad record first; the re-review measured 81 ms). In the session-1 bridge capture every layer record has one patch (492 records), and a record of one patch has nothing to carry.
    - A tripped budget leaves no trace of its own on the `Terrain:` line: the layer records count under `flat N` like those of any fallen square, and `without material:` lists only the records' own reasons (by reading `PlanTerrain` and `FormatTerrainText`).
    - The reflection test of the `Draw` block (`test_native_shaders.cpp`) holds each field's offset and size against `ClayConstants`, not its type.
    - `DecodeNormalMap` accepts one layout of the normal map.
    - Test gaps of Task 2 (`PrePassTest`: colour tiles after the first; `HasColourPass` on four values only), of Task 5 (rejection messages of `trace_ps_alpha` without the shader's hash; the constant-read loop of `FillMaterial` has no unit test) and of Task 8 (`DecodeDxt3aLevel` has no guard on its dimensions beyond its callers' cap of 8192).
    - `terrain_replay.py` finds a texture's dump by fetch dwords 1 and 2 and leaves out dword 0 (pitch and tiling): two layouts of one address would collide (none in the captures).
    - The driver does not poll between the gate's pass and the watch's first poll (under a second) nor between the last poll and the kill (milliseconds); its tests do not pin that the dead-man thread is armed outside test mode, nor the wall-clock term of the watch (the gate's is pinned).
13. The native ground against the offline replay (found in the lighting sub-project, plan Task 7; section 14, "Native light", "The dirt patch against the replay"): at the dirt patch by the bridge the native picture is 2% below the replay in red and green and 3% in blue, with the light off (0.982, 0.983, 0.972 once the unlit statement's facet shade, 0.950 there, is taken out) as with it on (0.979, 0.978, 0.969). The light is not the cause, nor mip filtering of the detail textures. A plausible share, not measured: the 11 / 11 / 10 bit target truncates toward zero at each of the six blended stores of a layered pixel. To settle it: the same comparison against a build with a 64-bit target, or the replay with the target's rounding applied after each layer.

**Final fix wave** (2026-10-07, after the whole-branch review; commits `3f0a484` code and tools, `b4ef5fe` the driver, and the commit that carries this text). What it changed in what this section describes:

- *Log lines.* The `Terrain:` line names terrain records without geometry (`no-positions`, `no-indices`) after `without material:`; the State line's `(overflow N)` counts a terrain record that found no pipeline for its material, once. No logged window of any run has had either.
- *Capture.* `RB_SURFACE_INFO` is read only for a draw whose state words were read and have alpha-to-mask on (`NeedsSurfaceInfo`); every other record carries 0, which the decoder never looked at. The side texture's fetch constant has a slot of its own in `FillTerrainMaterial`.
- *The plan of the ground.* The work budget and the rule for an adaptive record over the patch bound ("Native terrain material").
- *Tables.* Both generators refuse what they used to read loosely: a key outside the lists the committed tables use, a shader named twice in any spelling or written twice in the file, a slot or a base that is not a JSON integer in range, a layout other than `dot` or `combine`, an entry without `"manual": true` or evidence, an alpha recipe without its evidence; the bound of the steep block's small term (below 0.01) is one number for the generator, the replay tool and `MakeTerrainMaterialInputs`. Both generated tables are byte for byte what they were. The evidence strings of twenty entries that ended "pending (no game run allowed ...)" now say what was seen, or that the entry was never identified on screen.
- *Tests.* The `Draw` constant block of the vertex shader and of the four clay pixel shaders is reflected from the compiled shaders and held against `ClayConstants`; `BuildTerrainNormals` equals the replay tool's `SquareGrid` on the real normal map of game session 1 at all 665,856 grid points of its 2,304 squares (largest difference 1.11e-07; 242,035 points steeper than 30 degrees; `finalfix_tf17_grid.txt`; the test is skipped without `FABLE2_TF17_BIN` and `FABLE2_TF17_FC`).
- *Autoplay and the driver.* A plan entry at second 0 is dispatched at the first poll (before the wave it never ran). The native renderer is installed only about 1.5 s after the autoplay thread starts (`final_bridge_fable_2_277.log`: `[autoplay] armed` on line 1, `[native] native renderer installed` on line 33, 1.46 s later; 1.24 s in run 278), so a `VIEW=` entry at 0 s is dispatched, logged and ignored (`[native] SetView(...): native renderer not installed; ignored`), and a `DISC=` entry that early goes to `ArmDiscoveryNow`, which refuses while the capture layer is off (by reading; when that layer comes on was not traced); a button at 0 s is pressed. Setting the view it is in is not counted as a change. The driver checks the plan before the gate, requires the game's `[autoplay] armed` line after a run with a plan, has `-PlanShift`, prints its verdict as the last line, and words the gate's progress as observation (README).

## 14. Lighting

Sub-project 7 (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-native-renderer-lighting-design.md`, 2026-10-07). Evidence files of this section are in `out\native-evidence\lighting\` (git-ignored); the names below are files of that folder unless a path is given. The shaders' light formulas (plan Task 2: one section per light family, each checked against the microcode) are in [`lighting-formulas.md`](lighting-formulas.md).

### Pixel constants

Plan Task 1 (2026-10-07): where the GPU's shader constants come from at a draw, proved register by register against the emulated GPU's register file.

**Result.**

- *What the device's bank misses, and why.* The pixel bank at device + 0x1780 (section 8) holds what the game sets through the D3D constant setters; the draw builders upload it in groups of four registers whose pending bit is set. Two kinds of register reach the GPU without it:
  - `c80..c115`, the draw's light set, and `c0..c7`, the tiled scene's per-tile constants: the engine calls `LoadAluConstants` `0x8227CFC8`, which writes a `LOAD_ALU_CONSTANT` packet naming a constant buffer in guest memory, and the GPU reads the registers from that memory when it executes the packet;
  - a shader's own literals (`c248..c255` in these scenes): `GpuLoadShaders` uploads them from a table of the shader object with the same kind of packet (section 8, "Pixel shader microcode").

  The bank holds zero in all of them. In values, the bank differs from the GPU, in a draw whose pixel shader reads the register, at `c0`, `c80..c83`, `c87..c89`, `c94`, `c96`, `c105`, `c107`, `c109`, `c112` and the shaders' literals (`task1_bridge1_pconst_check_pbank.txt`, `task1_trees1_pconst_check_pbank.txt`: the same registers below `c248` in both scenes; literals `c252..c255` at the bridge, `c250..c255` by the trees); `c1..c7` and the other registers of `c80..c115` are loaded the same way and hold zero, a value no shader of the scenes reads, or what the bank holds too. That is the zero the research for this sub-project saw at `c0`, `c80`, `c81`, `c87`, `c88`, `c109`, `c112`.
- *The vertex bank has the same hole.* `LoadAluConstants` also loads vertex `c76..c107`, a light set in the pixel set's layout four registers lower (`c76` is light 0's direction or position, `c83` its colour), and vertex `c40..c43` per bin group; the vertex shaders' literals are uploaded like the pixel shaders'. "The vertex side", below.
- *The reader.* The capture follows both upload paths per guest thread (`ConstantLoads` in `light_block.h`; hooks on `LoadAluConstants` and on `SetPending_AluConstants`): per register, the last upload decides, the bank's or a buffer's. `ReadPixelConstant(reg, out)` in `capture.cpp` reads a register from there at a draw: the bank, or the buffer's memory (constant buffer object dword 6 is the address of its registers). It reads nothing for a register the draw's pixel shader loads itself (a literal). Discovery rows carry all 256 registers as it gives them (`"pfull"`). Since fix round 1 the uploads are followed only while somebody reads the result, in a frame with records or with a discovery capture requested or running; otherwise each upload hook only marks that an upload was skipped (`ConstantLoads::Skip`). That costs the reader only the registers a load can name (fix round 2, the controller's ruling). The ten call sites of `LoadAluConstants` pass their bank, first register and count as immediates (`kAluLoadSites` in `xdk_layout.h`; `fix2_alu_call_sites.txt`, from the generated code), so pixel `c0..c7` and `c80..c115` and vertex `c40..c43` and `c76..c107` are the only registers a buffer is ever the source of (`kBufferLoadRanges` in `light_block.h`, held against the call sites by a `static_assert`). Every other register is read from the bank whatever was followed: that is how `c19..c23` were read before this task, and what the first pair of captures proves for them. A load register is not given after skipped uploads until the first main scene whose uploads are all followed from its begin (the device's tiling flag, read at the followed upload hooks and the draws; `ConstantLoads::Observe`), and then only once an upload of it was followed, a load or the bank's upload of its group. A followed call that names a register outside the table makes it a load register from then on, is counted (`"untabled"` in the `"aluload"` rows) and logged once ("Limits").
- *Proof.* The reader with its last additions (a draw's own literals left out, the second writer of the bool word, the vertex rows) is proven on five frames of two `RUN UNCHECKED` runs, three at the bridge and two by the trees: 5,387 and 3,314 main-scene colour executions ("The later pair of captures", below). The two gated captures of eight frames (`RUN CLEAN`; 14,320 and 12,980 executions) prove the reader as it was before those additions. What fix rounds 1 and 2 changed after all four (the uploads followed only with records or discovery, the registers no load names read from the bank whatever was followed, the rules of a valid block) is tested as pure code and against the captures' rows, and was not run against the GPU ("Limits"). The method: every draw sampled, each capture paired draw by draw with a GPU-side log of the register file (`tools\xdk_sigmatch\pconst_check.py`, test `tests/test_pconst_check.py`; pairing by packet address as in section 13, "Render state"). The result: every register a draw's pixel shader reads has the reader's value in every colour-pass execution of the main scene. In the first two captures the shaders' own literals `c252..c255` are the exception (the reader did not leave them out yet); in the later pair there is none, in the main scene or outside it. The eleven registers of the light block (`LightBlock` in `light_block.h`: `c0`, `c19`, `c20`, `c21`, `c22`, `c23`, `c80`, `c87`, `c94`, `c109`, `c112`) are equal in all 14,320 such executions at the bridge and in all 12,980 by the trees, and again in 5,387 and 3,314 of the later pair, with one exception that is the game's and not the reader's: in 24 executions by the trees, and 6 in the later capture there (the first three main-scene draws of each frame, water, under bin select `0x2`) the GPU holds another light set in `c80..c115` than the reader gives, at a draw whose shader reads none of those registers ("Replays", below).
