Those paths are held by the unit tests alone (the six records of "A pair that was not checked" are inferred to be `pair-not-checked` from the log's counts; no row shows the word).

**Fix round 1 (2026-10-08): no run.** The review of this task found the captured values right in every row it checked and two defects of what the entry says and when the block is read: the element carried the fetch's destination swizzle where a reader needs source components ("Which components of an element a reader takes", above), and the block's retry was counted per draw ("The frame's light block"). Both are fixed in code that the six captures above can still judge offline, and no run was made: what the capture stores for a draw changed in two bytes of each element (the selector in place of the swizzle), which come from the table and the row's fetch, both in the captures; the replay of the selectors against the dumped streams is the check (above), and the suites and the release build are the rest (`task4_fix1_native_suite.txt`, `task4_fix1_python_suite.txt`, `task4_fix1_build.txt`). The generators now hold the two tables against each other (`gen_albedo_table.py` reads `vs-transforms.json` and refuses a pair whose vertex shader has no `"light"` or is of a kind the family cannot stand on; that a family is written `object` for `object-multi` it cannot see, and the test against `light_check.FAMILIES` holds that). The corrections to "In the game" above (the far terrain's three depth-only draws, the main-scene rows' rotation, the bridge's `c11.x` in capture B, the slow window, what the runs do not show) are of the text, each read again from the logs and captures named beside it. What a run would add, and none of this round needs: a `"lin"` row printing `"src"`.

**Follow-ups.**
1. A third side array for the probe sets. 192 of the entry's 312 bytes are `vs_const`, which only the `probe` draws use in full (54 a frame at the bridge, 155 by the trees) and the plants in one register. With the sets in an array of their own, indexed from the entry, the entry would be 120 bytes and the frame 157,248 bytes smaller at the bridge ((873 - 54) x 192) and 122,880 by the trees ((795 - 155) x 192). Not built: the capture's median did not rise with the 312-byte entry.
2. Enter the pairs the probe above found agreeing (`0x648B...` with the two skinned vertex shaders first) in `light_check.py`'s `FAMILIES` and in the table, with a capture that shows which pair the six records are.
3. A terrain draw's entry carries 11 bytes of use (the family, `c11.x`, the facing literal); if the entry is ever split, the terrain's part belongs with the patch.

### Normals

Plan Task 5a (2026-10-07): the light's per-vertex inputs in the geometry cache: vertex normals of the mesh draws (plain, bone-skinned, instanced), the terrain's normals for every terrain record, and the colour stream of vertex shader `0xECD6...`. **No shader reads them yet**: the clay pass asks the cache for them, so that they are resident and their cost is in the frame's figures, and binds none. The probe weights and the instance colours came with plan Task 5b ("Probe weights and instance colours" below). The decode and the transforms are pure code under unit tests (`src/native/render/normal_decode.h`, `tests/native/test_normal_decode.cpp`); what is said about real meshes rests on the stream dumps of the two C captures of "Light inputs" (`native_discovery_20261007_225337.jsonl` at the bridge, `_225705` by the trees; 8 frames each), read by `normal_survey.py` of the task's evidence (`out/native-evidence/lighting/task5a/`) and, for 45 exported meshes, by the C++ itself. What it does and costs in the game is in "In the game", at the end: two runs made by the controlling agent with the user at the machine (`RUN UNCHECKED`).

**The element.** Two formats hold a normal (`material.h`, `IsLightNormalFormat`):

- `FMT_10_11_11`, one dword at byte 8 of the vertex (objects `0xECD6...`, `0x79EA...`; trunks `0x475E...`; the skinned `0xD4D5...`, `0xA1F7...`): x in bits 0 to 10, y in bits 11 to 21, z in bits 22 to 31 of the dword read under the stream's endian (8in32 in every row: the guest's big-endian dword), each a signed field divided by its positive maximum (1023, 1023, 511) and at least -1. Source: the SDK's vertex fetch, `thirdparty/rexglue-sdk/src/graphics/pipeline/shader/interpreter.cpp`, `ExecuteVertexFetchInstruction` (`k_10_11_11`: widths 11, 11, 10 at offsets 0, 11, 22; signed and normalised: `max(-1, v / (2^(w - 1) - 1))`), and the same widths and offsets in `dxbc_translator_fetch.cpp`. The fetch's other signed mode (`kNoZero`, `(2v + 1) / (2^w - 1)`) is a bit of the instruction that `DecodeVertexFetches` does not keep; it would move a component by at most 1/1023, and the positions' decode ignores it the same way.
- `FMT_16_16_16_16_FLOAT`, at byte 8 of the 24-byte mesh vertex of the seven instancing shaders: the first three halves in memory.

**Which components are the normal.** Source components, **not** the fetch's destination swizzle: 10_11_11 source (x, y, z); halves source (y, x, w), which under the 8in32 pair swap of 16-bit components are memory halves 0, 1, 2. Since fix round 1 of Task 4 the element says so itself (`LightElement::source`, the table's `"source"`: "Light inputs", "Which components of an element a reader takes") and carries no swizzle; the reader selects through it and holds it against the format. The swizzle says where the fetch puts a component in the shader's register, and the shaders differ there while they use the same source components: `xyz_` (`0xE88`: `0xECD6...`, `0x79EA...`, `0x475E...`) and `_xyz` (`0x447`: the two skinned shaders) for the 10_11_11 element; `yxwz` (`0x4C1`: five plant shaders) and `yzxw` (`0x611`: `0x48D3...`, `0xFC4F...`) for the halves. `NormalSourceComponents` is what the reader knows of the two formats, and the cross-check of the table: an element whose selector is another order than its format's is `normal-unsupported`, never decoded either way. A fetch that does not deliver all three components into the register is `normal-unsupported` too (a shader that fetches two and derives the third has another normal than the stored one), decided at capture, where the fetch is (`LightSource`): such a draw has no light inputs. Evidence, three kinds:

1. *The microcode.* `light_check.py`'s vertex drivers take the normal in exactly this order for the plain object shaders, the trunks and the seven plant shaders (`_object_vertex`: the element's x, y, z; `_plant_vertex`: `_real` of the element, its y, x, w), and its 32 pairs agree in 400 of 400 trials (`task5a/light_check_all.txt`, this task's rerun). The two skinned shaders: "Skinned" below.
2. *Unit length.* The stored vector, taken this way and before any normalisation, has length 1 within 0.02 in **every** sampled vertex of all twelve vertex shaders: 110,960 of 110,960 vertex reads at the bridge and 118,203 of 118,203 by the trees (the vertices the rows' sampled indices name, over the 8 frames). The register's x, y, z under the swizzle is a unit vector in 1,473 of 5,318 and 817 of 2,407 vertices of `0x48D3...`, 86 of 664 and 41 of 868 of `0xFC4F...`, 4,103 of 13,691 and 3,976 of 14,626 of `0xD4D5...`, 628 of 1,717 and 2,034 of 6,505 of `0xA1F7...` (`normal_survey_bridge.txt`, `normal_survey_trees.txt`).
3. *The triangles.* For every distinct mesh draw with light inputs, the face normal of each sampled triangle against the mean of its three vertex normals, positions and normals built as the cache builds them (plain: the stream's space; skinned: after the bones; instanced: after the instance's rows). Mean |cos| over a shader's triangles, bridge / trees:

| Vertex shader | Meshes | Triangles | Mean \|cos\| | Per mesh: lowest, median | Other five component orders |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0xECD6...` (plain) | 120 / 120 | 25,147 / 31,855 | 0.962 / 0.973 | 0.807, 0.973 / 0.881, 0.976 | 0.32 to 0.58 |
| `0x79EA...` (plain) | 3 / 37 | 542 / 7,191 | 0.991 / 0.980 | 0.983, 0.996 / 0.863, 0.991 | 0.24 to 0.64 |
| `0x475E...` (trunks) | 19 / 25 | 4,709 / 6,415 | 0.926 / 0.929 | 0.860, 0.941 / 0.860, 0.941 | 0.38 to 0.60 |
| `0xD4D5...` (skinned, four bones) | 31 / 36 | 9,159 / 9,874 | 0.951 / 0.947 | 0.910, 0.949 / 0.892, 0.957 | 0.36 to 0.63 |
| `0xA1F7...` (skinned, one bone) | 4 / 18 | 1,003 / 3,908 | 0.986 / 0.880 | 0.945, 0.994 / 0.781, 0.976 | 0.30 to 0.60 |
| `0x8123...` (instanced) | 63 / 30 | 10,852 / 5,637 | 0.992 / 0.992 | 0.983, 1.000 / 0.975, 1.000 | 0.15 to 0.51 |
| `0xB636...` | 36 / 21 | 10,217 / 6,394 | 0.971 / 0.966 | 0.916, 0.976 / 0.933, 0.976 | 0.31 to 0.69 |
| `0x48D3...` | 17 / 6 | 2,758 / 1,235 | 0.993 / 0.993 | 0.975, 1.000 / 0.975, 1.000 | 0.09 to 0.49 |
| `0x6AD4...` | 8 / 4 | 2,149 / 1,117 | 0.979 / 0.973 | 0.937, 0.990 / 0.937, 0.990 | 0.36 to 0.63 |
| `0xFC4F...` | 2 / 2 | 368 / 514 | 0.991 / 0.986 | 0.988, 0.996 / 0.983, 0.988 | 0.26 to 0.77 |
| `0x33C0...` | 2 / 3 | 598 / 758 | 0.962 / 0.983 | 0.933, 0.983 / 0.975, 0.983 | 0.28 to 0.73 |
| `0x36B5...` | 1 / 0 | 361 / 0 | 0.937 | 0.937 | 0.37 to 0.62 |

   The five other orders of the three components read as the last column says (for the skinned shaders in the bind pose). The signed mean is within 0.002 of the mean of the absolute values in every plain and instanced shader and within 0.02 in the two skinned ones (`0xD4D5...` 0.946 / 0.939, `0xA1F7...` 0.986 / 0.861), with one sign throughout: the normals lie on one side of their triangles. A mesh reads below 0.9 where it is coarse and smoothly shaded: the lowest, a mesh of `0xA1F7...` by the trees (stream `1C805000`, 273 triangles), has triangles whose own three normals are far apart (mean of the lowest cosine between them 0.34) and still reads 0.83 in its bind pose against 0.40 to 0.59 for the other orders (`one_mesh_A1F7_1C805000.txt`). One line per mesh: `normal_survey_bridge_meshes.txt`, `normal_survey_trees_meshes.txt` (306 and 302 lines). No sampled vertex has a zero normal.

   The same comparison by the C++ (`tests/native/test_normal_decode.cpp` with `FABLE2_NORMALS_BIN`; 45 meshes exported by `normal_survey.py --export`, up to four per vertex shader, with their streams, skin and instance layouts): `DecodePositions`, `SkinPositions`, `ExpandInstances` against `DecodeNormals`, `SkinNormals`, `ExpandInstanceNormals`; mean of the meshes' mean |cos| 0.971, per vertex shader 0.937 to 0.999, the lowest mesh 0.784 (that mesh of `0xA1F7...`) (`real_bundles_cpp.txt`).

**Where a normal lives.** Where its positions live, for the same reason. The cache stores a plain mesh's positions as the stream holds them and the draw's rows are applied when it is drawn; so a plain mesh's normals are stored in the stream's own space, one buffer for every draw of the mesh however it is placed and turned, and the draw's world rotation (`LightInputs::world_rot`, `world_scale`) is applied when it is drawn (since plan Task 7 by the vertex shader, as the game's `normalize(M n)`: "Native light", "As drawn"). The key (`MeshNormalKey`, kind 9) is the stream, the element's offset and stride, its format and its source selector: nothing of the draw, and not the fetch's flags, which only admit the element (two shaders that fetch one stream's normal differently share the buffer: where a fetch puts the components in its register is not in the element). A skinned mesh's normals are stored after the bones, as its positions are (the key adds the skin layout, the palette's range and the stream of the bone words; the content hash the palette's and that stream's bytes); an instanced draw's as one normal per flat position after the instance's rows (kind 10; the key adds the instance set without its offset, which moves positions only). A normal in world space per draw would put the rotation into the key and make an entry per placed object.

**The transform.** One rule since plan Task 5b, by three rulings of the controlling agent (2026-10-08): **the game's own arithmetic**. Through the bones, through an instance's rows and through a draw's world rotation a normal goes as `normalize(M n)`, the matrix as it is, as the game's shaders compute it; under a matrix that scales unevenly or shears it then tilts as the game's does and is not perpendicular to the transformed surface. (Plan Task 5a built the instanced normals with the inverse transpose, `normalize(M^-T n)`: the cofactor matrix times the determinant's sign, `NormalMatrix`, which stays perpendicular. `NormalMatrix` and `RotateNormal(rot, n)`, the inverse transpose with (0, 0, 1) for a result without a direction, are still in `normal_decode.h` and under test; no builder and no draw uses them.) The cache's builders write **(0, 0, 0) for a vertex without a direction** (a zero or not finite element, a vertex without a position, a matrix that flattens it), so that nothing is made up; that convention is accepted: the game's own shader makes NaN of a zero normal and its saturating dot products make 0 of that (`lighting-formulas.md`, "Method"), and the shaders that light with these buffers treat a zero normal as unlit by the sun.

- *Instanced.* The game: `normalize(M n)` with M the 3 x 3 of the instance's rows, the rows the position goes through, in all seven plant vertex shaders. Read in the dumps: three `dp3` of the normal with the rows' xyz, each with the same components of the same row register as the position's `dp4` of that row, then the squared length, `rsq` and the product: `0x8123...` instr 22 to 24, 30, 35, 36 (position 26 to 28); `0x48D3...` and `0xFC4F...` 29 to 31, 37, 39, 40 (position 33 to 35); `0xB636...` 24, 29, 30, 34, 35, 37 (position 27, 28, 31; its tangent is made orthogonal to this normal afterwards, 39 and 40, not the normal to the tangent). By machine for all seven (`plant_normal_check.py` of plan Task 5b's evidence, on `ucode_eval.py`: 400 trials per shader with random rows that are no rotation, row lengths up to 57 to 1): interpolator `o1` equals `normalize(M n)` in 400 of 400 trials of each, largest component difference 0, where the inverse transpose of the same matrices is as far as cosine 0.001 to 0.013 from it (`plant_normal_check.txt`). `ExpandInstanceNormals` builds that form since plan Task 5b (ruling of 2026-10-08: the principle of the skinned ruling). In the captures the two forms do not differ: of the 2,603, 3,100 and 2,433 distinct copies the draws of the three captures of plan Task 7's runs name (bridge, third spot, trees; `native_discovery_20261008_055155`, `_055339`, `_055538`), **none** is off a rotation times one scale by more than 0.001 in a row's length or the cosine between two rows (largest 0.00073: the rows are half floats), and the largest angle between the two forms over every copy and every normal of its mesh is 0.052 degrees (`probe_instance_survey_*.txt`, `instance_forms_*.txt`). Before that, on the captures of plan Task 5a: the rows of every sampled copy a rotation times one scale within 0.0009, and the normal built then (the inverse transpose) equal to the game's in all 45,822 and 26,059 sampled vertices of the seven shaders (cosine 1.000000).
- *Skinned.* The closed formula of the two skinned shaders' `o1`, read from the dumps (`0xA1F7...` instr 16 to 21, 26 to 31, 34 to 41, 58; `0xD4D5...` 29 to 49, 50 to 55, 58 to 65, 82) and checked against the microcode (`skinned_normal_check.py`: 400 trials per shader with random bones, weights and constants, largest component difference 3.4e-14):

  ```
  B  = sum_j w_j * bone_j                (3 x 4, the matrix the position goes through: bone_skin.h)
  Nb = B3 n,  Tb = B3 t                  (B3: its 3 x 3; n the normal; t the 2_10_10_10 element at dword 6, source x, y, z)
  Nw = R Nb,  Tw = normalize(R Tb)       (R: rows c4..c6; these two shaders read no c7)
  o1 = normalize(Nw - Tw * (Tw . Nw))
  ```

  The normal through the blended matrix as it is, then made orthogonal to the tangent that went the same way. **The cache builds the first step, `normalize(B3 n)`, the game's own arithmetic, and not the second** (ruling of the controlling agent, 2026-10-08: the goal is the game's arithmetic; under a bone that scales, or a blend that is no rotation, the normal then tilts exactly as the game's `Nb` does; the tangent step needs an element the light inputs do not name and belongs with the normal maps, which are out of scope). The draw's rotation follows when the draw is drawn. For bones that are rotations and a stored normal perpendicular to its tangent the built normal is the game's `o1` (400 of 400 trials with one rigid bone, cosine 1.000000; in the streams `|n . t|` is at most 0.003 for `0xD4D5...` and 0.059 for `0xA1F7...`). On the captured meshes, with their palettes, against the game's `o1` (`normal_survey_bridge.txt`, `normal_survey_trees.txt`):

  | | Built: `R B3 n` | Not built: the inverse transpose, `R^-T B3^-T n` |
  |---|---|---|
  | `0xD4D5...`, bridge (13,651 vertices) | min 0.99935, mean 0.99999; below 0.99: 0 | min 0.99706, mean 0.99998; below 0.99: 0 |
  | `0xD4D5...`, trees (14,620) | min 0.97528, mean 0.99988; below 0.99: 11, below 0.95: 0 | min 0.93465, mean 0.99976; below 0.99: 108, below 0.95: 8 |
  | `0xA1F7...` (1,521 and 6,256) | min 0.99826, mean 0.99999 | min 0.99827, mean 0.99999 |

  Beyond cosine 0.999 (2.6 degrees) of the game's normal: 0 and 377 vertices of `0xD4D5...` for the built form (14 and 404 for the inverse transpose), 0 and 16 of `0xA1F7...`. What is left is the tangent step ("Follow-ups").
- *The draw's rotation.* `c4..c6` carry the object's scale and `c7.w` its reciprocal: their product is orthonormal within 5e-7 in every sampled row (`c7.w` from 0.67 to 1.53 for the trunks, 1 for the objects), so the game's `o1 = R n * c7.w` is a rotation of the normal there. That is not assumed: `ClassifyRotation(rot, scale)` says per draw whether the product is a rotation, mirrored (a mirrored object or a negative `c7.w`), skewed (not orthonormal after the scale: the game's normal is then not perpendicular, `RotateNormal`'s is) or degenerate, and the pass counts the kinds of the object and trunk records it prepared (the log line below).

**Terrain normals for every terrain record.** The three terrain vertex shaders read their normal map (tf17, `k_DXN`) at the height coordinate of the vertex (`0xC30A...` and `0xFB68...` instr 25 with the coordinate of the heightmap fetch of instr 20; `0x5003...` instr 27 with that of instr 18) and form `(2x - 1, 2y - 1, sqrt(|1 - x^2 - y^2|))`, the normal their pixel shaders light with. Until now the map's fetch constant was captured, and `(n.x, n.y)` built, only for a layer whose pixel shader has the steep-ground block. Now the capture reads the fetch constant for every terrain draw (one more 24-byte read of the device's shadow per terrain draw) and keeps it in `TerrainMaterialInputs::normal_fetch` when it is a texture fetch constant; without the block the map is the light's alone, so a draw without one keeps its material and has no normals. `BuildTerrainNormals` and `GeometryCache::TerrainNormals` build the grid points' `(n.x, n.y)` for any valid terrain record, a draw of the adaptive builder included (its patches are larger, and its grid samples the map as it samples the heights); the key (`TerrainNormalKey`) is unchanged, so the layers of a square, with or without the block, share one buffer.

**The colour stream.** `0xECD6...` reads an 8_8_8_8 element of a stream of its own at the vertex index plus vertex `c20.x` (`LightInputs::colour_first`) and raises it to 2.2. The buffer (`GeometryCache::Colours`, kind 11) holds, for each vertex of the draw's position buffer, element `colour_first + v` of the stream as four bytes red, green, blue, alpha, as stored (the power is the draw's, `colour_power`). Red, green and blue are the element's source z, y, x (a D3DCOLOR in guest memory is the bytes A, R, G, B): the element's selector, the table's `zyxw`, held against `ColourSourceComponents` (another selector gives no colours). Bounded by the stream: a read past its size fails the buffer, and a buffer that would hold fewer colours than the draw's indices name (its largest vertex plus one, `LightVertexNeed`: the capture's own rule for the stream, since plan Task 7; before that the rule was every vertex of the position stream, stricter than the capture's and with no slack in most rows: meshes share a colour stream, which ends where its last mesh's vertices end) is not built (`light-unknown` for that record). The shader reads the buffer only for a vertex below the number of colours it holds (`ClayConstants::light_count`). In all 1,920 rows of `0xECD6...` in the two captures the stream holds at least as many colours from `c20.x` on as the position stream has vertices (`lin_survey_bridge_trees.txt`). The key is the stream, the element's place and selector, `colour_first` and the count: meshes that share a colour stream at different offsets are different entries, draws of one mesh share theirs, and a mesh drawn with two colour streams is two entries; the content hash is the stream's.

**Counted, not assumed.** A record with light inputs whose normals or whose kind's second buffer (the colours of `0xECD6...`; since plan Task 5b the probe weights and sets, and an instanced draw's colours: "Probe weights and instance colours" below) cannot be built keeps its material and is counted: `normal-unsupported` (an element no reader decodes: another format, endian or sign, a selector that is not its format's; for terrain a slot without a `k_DXN` map; a fetch that does not deliver the three components is the same reason, given at capture, and such a draw is not among the lit records here) or `light-unknown` (a stream that cannot be read or is too short, a bone word outside its stream, a second buffer that was refused, a failed upload), by vertex shader. Every 300 frames, with textures on: `[native] clay: Light inputs: ready R of L lit records | world rotation: rotation a, mirrored b, skewed c, degenerate d [| skewed by vs {...}] [| light-unknown n by vs {...}] [| normal-unsupported n by vs {...}] [| probe or instance buffer refused: element e, stream s, probe-set p, value v, upload u]` ("lit": prepared records with light inputs and their material; "ready": their buffers are built: for a terrain record its normals, for a mesh record its normals **and** its kind's second buffer; the last part lists only the causes that occurred, and its records are among the `light-unknown` ones). The buffers go through the cache's index like every other, so its byte budget and the log's resident figure count them, and uploads and hits include them.

**Probe weights and instance colours** (plan Task 5b, 2026-10-08). The second per-vertex input of the two vertex kinds without a colour stream, built and resident like the normals; **no shader reads them and no draw binds them yet** (plan Task 8 lights with them), so nothing is drawn differently. Pure code: `normal_decode.h` (at its end) under `tests/native/test_normal_decode.cpp`; the keys in `clay_logic.h`. What is said about real streams rests on the three captures of plan Task 7's runs (`native_discovery_20261008_055155.jsonl` bridge, `_055339` third spot, `_055538` trees; 8 frames each; `RUN UNCHECKED`), read by `probe_instance_survey.py` of the task's evidence (`out/native-evidence/lighting/task5b/`).

- *Probe* (vertex shaders `0x79EA...`, `0xD4D5...`, `0xA1F7...`, `0x475E...`; `lighting-formulas.md`, "Objects", `probe_colour`). Per vertex of the mesh the four weights (the 32_32_32_32_FLOAT element of the weights stream, 16 bytes a vertex, source x, y, z, w = memory order) and the set index `a0` (0, 4 or 8: the offset of the vertex's probe set from `c28`). The twelve probe constants `c28..c39` are the draw's (`LightInputs::vs_const`) and are in no buffer: one entry per mesh however many objects use it, and for the two skinned shaders one entry whatever the palette holds (the weights are the bind mesh's; they are not rebuilt with the bones).
  - *The set index* is the position element's source z (`a0_comp` 2 in all four table entries): of the 16_16_16_16_FLOAT position under the stream's endian 8in32 the fourth half in memory. Each shader truncates it and loads its address register (`0x79EA...` instr 28, 29: `truncs r2.x`, `maxas a0, r2.x`, `r2.x` being source z of a `zxwy` fetch; `0x475E...` `r1.w` after `wxyz`; `0xD4D5...` `r7.w` and `0xA1F7...` `r1.w` after `yxwz`). The record's position layout carries the table's swizzle override, which drops that component, so the reader takes the element's source components as they are, whatever the swizzle. **Every vertex of every distinct position stream** of the probe draws holds exactly 0, 4 or 8 there, not only the sampled ones: 40,536 vertices at the bridge (0: 10,008; 4: 18,046; 8: 12,482), 44,543 at the third spot, 77,124 by the trees, no other value. The reader therefore refuses a mesh with **any** other value in any vertex (`probe-set`; no partial buffer, nothing truncated into a set): a value in between is taken as the sign of another element.
  - *The weights* are finite and at most 1 in magnitude in every vertex of every distinct weights stream (162,144, 178,172 and 310,008 values). A weight that is not finite or larger than 1,024 (`kProbeWeightMax`, the bound of the light's constants) refuses the mesh (`value`): no vertex is given made-up weights. The weights stream holds exactly as many vertices as the position stream in every row.
  - *The buffer* (`GeometryCache::Probe`, kind 12, built by `BuildProbe`): two regions of one buffer, the weights as `float4` a vertex from byte 0 and the set indices as one `uint` a vertex from the next multiple of 256 bytes on (`ProbeSetOffset(count)`: the RHI's alignment of a bind offset). Two regions, not one 20-byte element, so that each is read with an element no larger than a position's 16 bytes: the rule of the light registers ("Native light", "Buffers and textures") is that whatever stands in for a buffer holds every element a shader could load, and the stand-in is the draw's positions. Key (`ProbeKey`): the weights' stream, element and selector, the position stream and what places the set index in a vertex (offset, stride, format, pair swap, component); nothing of the draw, the skin or the palette; content hash of the two streams.
- *Instance* (the seven plant vertex shaders; "Instanced plants"). Per flat position the colour of its copy: the 8_8_8_8 element at byte 24 of the 28-byte instance record, red, green and blue = source z, y, x (the bytes in memory are one byte no fetch delivers, then R, G, B; that first byte is 254 in every captured copy), as one `uint` with red in the low byte and the fourth byte zero, as stored (the power `c18.w` is the frame's). `GeometryCache::InstanceColours`, kind 13, `ExpandInstanceColours`: index for index the order of the flat positions. An index the constants map to no copy has no position either and gets zero; a copy the draw names whose colour lies past the stream refuses the buffer (`stream`: no colour is made up for a vertex that has a position). Key (`InstanceColourKey`): the element with its selector and the instance set exactly as the flat normals have it (`InstanceNormalHash`: not the offset), so a draw's colours live as long as its normals; content hash of the element's stream.
- *Selectors held against the format*, as for the normal and the colour stream: `WeightsElementSupported` requires the table's selector to be `WeightsSourceComponents` (x, y, z, w), `InstanceColourElementSupported` requires `InstanceColourSourceComponents` (z, y, x; three components: the colour stream's four are refused), `ProbeSetSupported` requires the table's `a0_comp` to be the component `ProbeSetSourceComponent` knows for the position's layout (source z of halves under the pair swap; any other layout is refused). A well-formed wrong entry gives no buffer (`element`), never the bytes read another way. The weights' order rests on the microcode: every probe `dp4` pairs the fetched register with the constant under one swizzle or its exact undoing (`0x79EA...` 30 to 32 and `0x475E...` 66 to 68: fetched `xyzw`, `dp4 r.yxzw, c.yxzw`; `0xD4D5...` 76 to 78 and `0xA1F7...` 52 to 54: fetched `wzxy`, `dp4 r.wzyx, c.yxzw`, and register `wzyx` of a `wzxy` fetch is source y, x, z, w). The test decodes real bytes of each of the four probe shaders' weights and position element, and of all seven plant shaders' instance colour, through the committed table's entry and its row's fetch (bytes of capture `055155`, frame 1, named per element in the test), so an entry of any of the eleven that named other components fails there.
- *Ready, and refused.* A probe or an instanced record is "ready" in the `Light inputs:` line only with its normals and this buffer (before plan Task 5b: its normals). A refused buffer makes the record `light-unknown` (it keeps its material) and is counted by cause in the line's last part: `element`, `stream`, `probe-set`, `value`, `upload`.
- *Cost, by reading* (measured: "In the game" at the end of this list): one more cache lookup a frame for every probe and every instanced record; the instance stream's and the position stream's hashes are the frame's memo, the weights streams are hashed once a frame each (0.65 MB at the bridge, 1.2 MB by the trees). Resident: 20 bytes a vertex of a probe mesh in two regions rounded to 256 bytes each, and 4 bytes a flat position of an instanced draw beside the 16 of its position and the 12 of its normal (the instanced normals were 3.0 MB at the bridge and 1.9 MB by the trees in plan Task 5a's runs: a third of that).
- *The registers plan Task 8 needs, and why nothing is bound here.* The instance colour is the stream colour's element (one `uint` a vertex, red in the low byte), so it goes to the colour register t10 with no change of the layout: the shader is told the kind by `light_mode`. The probe's set index is one `uint` a vertex too and goes to t10 as well (a probe draw has no colour), bound from `ProbeSetOffset(count)`. The four weights are a `float4`: a buffer bound by address is read with the stride of the shader's declaration, so they cannot share t10 short of reading five words a vertex through a 4-byte declaration, which would break the 16-byte rule above; they need a register of their own, **t11** (`float4`, vertex stage), the twelfth and last binding parameter of the backend (`kMaxBindingParams` 12; root words 54 to 56 of 64). It is **not** added here: no shader declares it, and a binding parameter belongs in the same change as the shader that reads it and the tests that hold both (`kClayBindings`, `LightBufferSources` with a fourth source, shader tests 153 to 162: 156 holds "no t11" today). `Prepared::probe` / `probe_count` and an instanced record's `Prepared::colours` are filled and bound by no draw; these records are not lit, their `light_mode` is 0 and their three light registers hold their own positions, as before.
- *Not there.* The plants' bent normal (`lighting-formulas.md`, "Instanced plants") needs, besides the built normal, the copy's up axis (the third column of its rows, normalised) and the vertex's height in the copy's space per flat position, which no buffer holds: four floats, which would fit the same `float4` register as the probe weights. The probe constants of a draw (three sets of a colour and three rows) are 45 floats, 180 bytes: with cbuffer `Object`'s 80 they do not fit a 256-byte slot of the frame's constant buffer.
- *In the game* (2026-10-08 06:49 to 06:56; the build of 06:43:47 that this text describes). Four runs made by the controlling agent with `tools\drive_game.ps1 -UserPresent -BootSave Hero000`: verdict `RUN UNCHECKED`, exit 0, for each; the display state of plan Task 6's and 7's runs (1024 x 768, shots of 1296 x 759). **Follow-ups.**
1. *The tangent step of the skinned normal* is not built in this sub-project: `o1 = normalize(Nw - Tw * (Tw . Nw))` with `Tw` the tangent (the 2_10_10_10 element at dword 6 of the skinned vertex) through the same blended matrix and rotation. The native skinned normal is `normalize(R B3 n)`. What that leaves, measured on the two captures against the game's `o1`: nothing at the bridge beyond cosine 0.999 (13,651 vertices of `0xD4D5...`, minimum 0.99935; 1,521 of `0xA1F7...`, minimum 0.999997); by the trees 377 of 14,620 vertices of `0xD4D5...` beyond 0.999, 11 beyond 0.99 (8 degrees), none beyond 0.95, minimum 0.97528 (12.8 degrees), and 16 of 6,256 of `0xA1F7...` beyond 0.999, minimum 0.99826. It needs the tangent element in `LightInputs` (a table field and a fetch) and one more decode beside the normal's; it belongs with the normal maps, which read the same tangent.
2. The fetch's second signed mode (`kNoZero`) is not kept by `DecodeVertexFetches` ("The element").
3. A skinned record's positions and normals are skinned in two passes, each decoding the palette; one pass over the bones for both would halve that work if the run's `decode` figure asks for it.

### Linear target

Plan Task 6 (2026-10-08). Code: `clay_logic.h` "Linear target and output mapping" (`OutputMap`, `ClayLinear`, `ClayLinearShaded`, `kClayClearLinear`, `ClayLight`, `ClayExposure`, `FormatTargetText`), `clay_pass.cpp` (`Ensure`, the end of `Render`), `fable2_native_shaders.h` (`clay_linear`, `terrain_out`, `kOutputMapPs`), the switch `--fable2_native_light=off|on` in `fable2_native_render.cpp`, and `ViewFrameLog` in `native_render_state.h` (the `[native] frames:` log line, below). Tests: `test_clay_logic.cpp` cases 390 to 417, `test_native_shaders.cpp` codes 35 to 38, 42 to 46 and 60 to 79, `test_native_render_state.cpp` codes 24 to 34. Evidence: `task6\`, and the runs' files `task6_*` beside it.

**What the pass does now.** It has three targets of 1120 x 720, created and destroyed together: linear colour (`R11G11B10_FLOAT`: 11-bit floats for red and green, a 10-bit float for blue, no sign, no alpha), depth, and the 8-bit target the composite views sample, which is now the *mapped* target. Every draw renders into the linear target with its captured state as before. At the end of each `Render` one full-screen triangle (`kFullscreenVs`, `kOutputMapPs`) writes every pixel of the mapped target from the linear one, texel for texel: `display = sqrt(saturate(linear * E))`, alpha 1. `E` is one number per frame (`ClayExposure`), 1 with light off, and 1 with light on until the lit shaders exist and it is calibrated. The composite is unchanged: it samples the mapped target as it sampled the 8-bit clay target. The linear format is the same for every pipeline of the pass, so `PipelineKey` has no field for it and the pipeline cache holds the same keys as before; the mapping's own pipeline, layout and shaders are outside the cache and are not counted in the State line's `P pipelines`.

**What each shader writes** (`d` is what it wrote into the 8-bit target before; the mapping with `E = 1` returns `d`):

| Shader | Before (display) | Now (linear) |
|---|---|---|
| flat clay (`kClayPs`, and the flat branch of `kClayTexturedPs`) | `base * (0.35 + 0.65 f)` | `saturate(base * (0.35 + 0.65 f))^2` |
| textured (`kClayTexturedPs`) | `t.rgb * (0.75 + 0.25 f)` | `saturate(t.rgb * (0.75 + 0.25 f))^2` |
| terrain layer | `tf0 * (0.75 + 0.25 f)` | `saturate(tf0^2 * (0.75 + 0.25 f)^2)` |
| terrain layer, steep-ground block | `sqrt(mix) * (0.75 + 0.25 f)` | `saturate(mix * (0.75 + 0.25 f)^2)`, `mix = s * tf0^2 + (1 - s) * (wy * tf13(o1)^2 + wx * tf13(o0)^2)` |
| far terrain | `tf13 * (0.75 + 0.25 f)` | `saturate(tf13^2 * (0.75 + 0.25 f)^2)` |
| underlay (`2F8A`) | `tf13 * S * (0.75 + 0.25 f)` | `saturate(tf13^2 * S * (0.75 + 0.25 f)^2)`, `S = sat(d2 * c31.x + c31.y)` |

`f` is the facet term. *The shade's space, derived:* the 8-bit target held `D = c * shade` for a colour `c`, and the mapping returns the root of what the linear target holds, so the linear value must be `D^2 = c^2 * shade^2`: the shade multiplies the display value, that is, its square multiplies the linear one. A shade applied to the linear value as it is would give `c * sqrt(shade)`: white flat clay facing away would read 0.59 instead of 0.35 (test case 401). `c^2` is the game's own `A = albedo^2` (and `tf0^2`, `tf13^2`: section 13, "Terrain"). The underlay's factor `S` multiplies the linear value because the game multiplies its whole (linear) colour by it; that is the one place where a shader's own value, not only a blend, differs from the materials picture (`S` against `sqrt(S)` in display terms, inside the cross-fade ring).

**Alpha.** Not squared. The alpha test and alpha-to-mask read it exactly as before and come before the output. Where it is written for the blend it is clamped to [0, 1] in the shader: the 8-bit target clamped a shader's output before blending and a float target does not, so an alpha over 1 (the scale constants of a recipe are the game's) would have made the inverse factor negative. The linear target has no alpha channel, and nothing needs one: the RHI's blend factors are zero, one, source alpha and inverse source alpha (`RhiFactor`; the source alpha is the shader's output, not the target's), the mapping writes alpha 1, and the composite reads the mapped target's colour. A draw whose colour mask is alpha only writes nothing, which shows as it did.

**What differs from the materials picture: blends are of linear values.** With one draw's value in a pixel the mapped result is the old one, up to the target's rounding (below). Where a blend mixes two values it is now the game's arithmetic:
- *Terrain layers.* `sqrt(sum(w_i * c_i^2))` where it was `sum(w_i * c_i)`: never darker, equal where one layer has the whole weight or the layers have one colour, and brighter in between (0.3 against 0.6 at half weight: 0.474 where it was 0.45, six levels; test cases 402, 403). This removes the darker transition bands section 13 lists as an approximation.
- *Weights that do not add up to 1.* A pixel near the eye (where the underlay's factor `S` is 0) whose layers' weights add up to `W < 1` read `W * c` and reads `sqrt(W) * c`. From session 1's captures (`materials\session1_bridge_weight_sums_f2.txt`, `..._trees_...`): the sum is below 0.98 at 13 of 7,803 grid points of the squares whose first layer replaces and at 644 of 18,785 of the squares over the underlay at the bridge (48 of 8,959 and 513 of 19,941 by the trees), below 0.9 at 136 and at 9 + 79, least 0.857 at the bridge and 0.786 by the trees (there: 8% and 13% brighter than before). In both captures the sum never exceeds 1.000 at a grid point, and then it cannot between grid points either (each weight is a clamped affine function of the cell's bilinear value, convex below the upper clamp, so a pixel's sum is at most the bilinear mean of its four grid points' sums), so the linear sum of a pixel's layers stays at or below its brightest layer and at or below 1: the float target, which no longer clamps at 1, holds no terrain sum the game's would not.
- *The cross-fade ring* (48 to 64 units from the eye): `sqrt(S * far^2 + (1 - S) * near^2)` where it was `S * far + (1 - S) * near`: brighter where the far texture and the layers differ.
- *Alpha-blended draws* (source alpha, inverse source alpha): brighter between the two values, by the same inequality. *Additive draws* (one or source alpha, one): `sqrt(s^2 + d^2)` where it was `min(1, s + d)`: darker. A sum over 1 stays in the target and the mapping clamps it at the end.

**No NaN, nothing negative, nothing over 1 from a draw.** A NaN in a float target does not go away: every later blend into that pixel keeps it. What the shaders can produce: the albedo, terrain and side textures are UNORM formats (`texture_cache.cpp`: BC1, BC2, BC3, 8888, R8), so a sample is in [0, 1]; the facet term is `saturate(abs(dot(n, l)))` of a normalised cross product that is NaN on a degenerate triangle, and compiles to `min(|x|, 1)`, which returns 1 for a NaN (a D3D `min` returns the operand that is a number): the shade is finite, as it was; the colour division by alpha is `saturate(t.rgb / max(t.a, 1/255))`. The one unbounded term is the steep-ground mix (`1 / (|n.x| + eps + |n.y|)`, finite while the shader's literal `eps` is positive: 0.0001 in the table), and its output keeps a real clamp. Compiled (`test_native_shaders.cpp` code 69): the layer shader ends in `mul_sat o0.xyz`, the textured one clamps its colour (`mul_sat`) and its written alpha (`mov_sat`), the far one keeps `min(x, 1)` (the compiler proves its value is not negative) and flat clay keeps no clamp (the compiler proves [0, 1]: an 8-bit colour over 255 times a shade in [0.35, 1]). *The target has no sign bit.* By the format's definition a negative value is stored as 0, minus infinity as 0, plus infinity as infinity and a NaN as a NaN (DirectXMath's `XMStoreFloat3PK` is the reference conversion; what this GPU does was not measured, since nothing writes such a value). No shader and no blend relies on storing a negative: every term of a blend is a product of a value that is not negative (a shader output clamped to [0, 1], or what the target holds) and a factor in [0, 1] (zero, one, the clamped alpha, one minus the clamped alpha), and the one other operation is `min`. The mapping does not rely on any of this: `v = (v > 0) ? min(v, 1) : 0` before the root (compiled as a comparison and a mask, code 68), so a negative value, a NaN and `0 * infinity` give 0, +infinity gives 1, and the root only ever sees [0, 1] (`OutputMap`, cases 390 to 393). A NaN that did reach the linear target would show as a black pixel, not spread by the mapping.

**The format: `R11G11B10_FLOAT`, chosen for the frame rate, and what it costs in precision** (controller's ruling, 2026-10-08: frame rate first). The first build of this task used `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`. Six runs of one experiment build (binary of 02:11:26 with temporary switches for the target's format and for where the mapping runs, since removed; `RUN UNCHECKED`, made by the controlling agent with `-UserPresent`, 02:14 to 02:22; bridge plan; per-view lines of 150 frames, `task6\x_frames_table.txt`) gave, in whole windows from the first view switch on:

| Run | Log | View off | Native, split, overlay |
|---|---|---|---|
| renderer not installed (`--fable2_native_render=false`) | `task6_x_control_fable_2_298.log` | 29.5, 29.0, 29.3, 29.5 fps (`[frame] guest`; frame median 33.5 to 33.7 ms) | - |
| 8-bit target, mapping in the composite (the old pass's load; its picture is banded, timing only) | `task6_x_rgba8c_fable_2_301.log` | 29.6 fps (33.8 ms) | 26.9 to 27.7 fps (mean 36.1 to 37.2 ms) |
| `R11G11B10_FLOAT`, mapping as a step (**what is built**) | `task6_x_r11_fable_2_299.log` | 29.3 fps (34.1 ms) | 26.6 to 28.0 fps (35.8 to 37.6 ms) |
| `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`, mapping as a step | `task6_x_rgba16f_fable_2_300.log` | 29.3 fps (34.1 ms) | 25.4 to 26.0 fps (38.4 to 39.4 ms) |
| `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`, mapping in the composite | `task6_x_composite_fable_2_303.log` | 29.1 fps (34.4 ms) | 25.9 to 26.6 fps (37.6 to 38.6 ms) |

(A sixth run, `R11G11B10_FLOAT` with the mapping in the composite, ended after 5 s with a guest access violation at the save's load, `task6_x_r11c_fable_2_302.log`: `Unhandled guest access violation: read of guest 0x00000004 ... on thread 0xF800002C` in the millisecond of `[boot-save] loading Hero000`, on the game's own thread, 3.3 s after the pass had created its targets and pipelines without an error line. The same fault on the same guest thread is in two logs of builds that had no float target, `fable_2_001.log` and `fable_2_027.log`, the second with the renderer disabled. It was not repeated, and the path it ran is deleted.) Read: these runs were made in another display state than the runs that read 30.0 (next paragraph), and in it the game is slow by itself (without the renderer it reads 29.0 to 29.5 fps where run 279 read 30.0, so a frame sits at the pacing boundary and anything more takes longer); with the view off the pass costs nothing (29.1 to 29.6, as the control); in these six runs the 32-bit float target with the mapping step costs what the old pass's load costs, the 64-bit target 2 to 3 ms a frame more, and folding the mapping into the composite gains about 1 ms of that. Why a 64-bit target would cost that much on this GPU (an RTX 5080) is not established, and the later runs of the final build do not confirm the size of it ("Frame rate of the final build", below: one configuration differs by 1.5 to 2 ms between two runs). **Acceptance for this task** (ruling): no slower than the old-like pass in the same display state; the 30.0 fps of success criterion 7 was not shown in this display state and is pending for the normal one (next paragraph).

**The display state of runs 294 to 303, and what is pending for the normal one.** Established (the controlling agent, 2026-10-08 about 02:45: `System.Windows.Forms.Screen` and `Win32_VideoController`): the machine had one display of 1024 x 768 at 60 Hz, which is what Windows falls back to when the monitor is off or disconnected; the runs that read 30.0 were made on the user's monitor (240 Hz, scale 150%). The logs and shots show the two states: the runs that read 30.0 (every run with shots from 277 to 293 but 280) have a game window of 1942 x 1136 (a client area of 1920 x 1080: the 1280 x 720 output scaled by 1.5), and their logs (277, 279, 283, 290, 292 read) have the present gate's line `suppressed N host presents` about once a second with N about 30; the shots of runs 294 to 301 and 303 are 1296 x 759 (a client area of 1280 x 720) and their logs (294, 296, 298, 299 read) have that line once or never, with N = 0, as has run 280 (2026-10-07 18:05, a gated run: 1296 x 759; 24.2 and 26.1 fps, with Task 1's diagnostic on). That the display state is why the frames are slower is inference: it is the only known difference. Two consequences. The frame times of the two states are not comparable, so this task's frame-rate acceptance is within one state (above), and **pending for the normal display state** (one bridge and one trees run with the rules' plans when the monitor is back): 30.0 fps in the native-view `frames:` windows with a renderer-off control at 30.0, and the native shots against runs 292 / 293 pixel for pixel. And a shot of one state cannot be laid over a shot of the other; the comparisons below bring the 1942 x 1136 baselines to 1280 x 720 by inverting the presenter's scaling ("Comparison with the materials picture"). The idle gate does not pass in the fallback state: two gated launches of this task (02:42 and 02:49, `task6f_bridge_refused1_driver_output.txt`, `task6f_bridge_driver_output.txt`) were refused after 420 s with `another program is fullscreen or presenting now` throughout (`SHQueryUserNotificationState`), with nobody at the machine.

*Precision, as arithmetic* (test cases 397, 398, 400, 416; each line is printed by the test; "nearest / toward zero" are the two ways a GPU may round when it writes the target):

| | Red and green (6 mantissa bits) | Blue (5 mantissa bits) | For comparison: a half-float channel |
|---|---|---|---|
| The 256 levels of the 8-bit target, squared, stored, mapped: levels that come back as themselves | 245 / 193 | 187 / 123 | 256 / 256 |
| Largest error, in levels | 1 / 1 | 1 / 3 (three levels off: two levels, 183 and 199, toward zero only) | 0 / 0 |
| 2,048 shaded values (a level times a facet shade): one, two, three levels off | 244, 0, 0 / 559, 7, 0 | 525, 7, 0 / 795, 156, 15 | (the first build's test: 145 of 4,096 one level off over both rounding rules, none farther) |
| Five equal additive layers of one colour, stored after each blend: largest error | 2 / 3 | 3 / 6 | - |

The errors are in the upper half of the range, not at the dark end: a float keeps a fixed share of the value (1/64 or 1/32), which after the root is the most levels where the value is large, and blue steps at other places than red and green, so a smooth bright gradient can show a faint tint. Dark values are exact in both widths. *In the game* (run 299 against run 300, the same plan and second with the two formats, 917,691 pixels outside what moves; `task6\x_channels_300_299.txt`): red 85.3% of the pixels equal, 14.7% one level off, none more than two; green the same (85.5%, 14.5%); blue 78.5% equal, 20.7% one off, 0.75% two off, largest 3; the mean is 0.15 (red, green) and 0.22 (blue) of a level darker. No pixel outside what moves differs by more than 3 levels, against the 8 that success criterion 2 counts from. **Limitation, recorded:** the native picture is within one level in red and green and within three in blue of what a 64-bit target shows.

Memory at 1120 x 720 (806,400 pixels): the linear target is 3,225,600 bytes (3.1 MB; `R16G16B16A16_FLOAT` would be 6,451,200, 6.2 MB); with the mapped target (3.1 MB) and the depth (3.1 MB) the pass's targets are 9.2 MB where they were 6.2 MB.

**Clear colour.** The 8-bit target was cleared to (0.08, 0.08, 0.1), and the shots show (20, 20, 25) where nothing is drawn (19.73% of the client area of `task5a_bridge_shot_42s.png`; 0.1 * 255 is 25.5 and the shot has 25: by inference that GPU's clear rounds it down). The linear target is cleared to the values nearest those levels' squares that its channels hold exactly, 101 / 16384 for red and green and 39 / 4096 for blue (`kClayClearLinear`; cases 405, 406, 415, 417), so the clear does not depend on rounding; they map to 20.02, 20.02 and 24.88, the three levels. Run 299's shot has (20, 20, 25) there (with the squares themselves as the clear, which that build used).

**Lifetime of the targets.** They have one size and do not follow the window (the composite scales, as before), so a resize creates nothing. A view switch creates nothing either. They are created in `ClayPass::Ensure`, all three with the linear target's view, in three cases: the first use; another device (`dev != device_`: the old objects are forgotten, not destroyed through the new device, the mapping's layout included); and the retry after a failed `Ensure` (F6 clears the failure latch; `DestroyObjects` destroys whatever the failed attempt created, the mapping's pipeline, shaders and view included). `targets_generation_` is bumped in the same place, and `RenderClay` redraws when it changed even if the scene did not. A new texture may hold anything (the RHI creates it with `GetHeapFlagCreateNotZeroed`), so order matters: the mapping runs at the end of every `Render`, after the clear and the draws of that same call, and nothing else writes or reads the two colour targets, so the mapped target is never older than its linear target and the composite, which runs after `Render` in the same callback, never samples a mapped target that was not written. A view switched on shows the emulated frame until the first scene is published (`RenderClay`), as before.

**Frame times per view: the `[native] frames:` line.** The SDK's `[frame] guest` line is a window of 300 frames whatever the view, so a plan that switches views every six seconds has no window of one view (which misled the first reading of runs 294 to 297). The renderer now logs `[native] frames: view V (window): 150 frames, mean M ms (F fps), median, p90, max` for every 150 guest frames in one view, and `view V (until the switch): N frames, ...` for the stretch a view switch ends (not below 10 frames); the frame of the switch is counted for neither view. It is taken at the guest frame poll (`PollFrame`), only while the renderer is installed, and costs a timestamp and one stored number a frame (`ViewFrameLog`, pure, in `native_render_state.h`). It was the experiment build's diagnostic (`x-frames:` in the logs of runs 299 to 303) and is kept.

**Comparison with the materials picture** (success criterion 2). The shots of the materials picture (runs 292 / 293 and, for the third spot, run 275 of the materials sub-project) are 1942 x 1136 and this task's are 1296 x 759 (above), so the baselines are brought to 1280 x 720 first (`task6\resample.py`): the presenter's scaling is modelled as plain bilinear sampling by 1.5 and the 1280 x 720 picture is the least-squares solution of "scaled(x) = shot", along rows and then columns. The model checks itself: scaled up again, the solution reproduces `task5a_bridge_shot_42s.png` with a mean residual of 0.21 level and a largest of 0.9 (`resample_check.txt`), so the presenter is bilinear and nothing else. The resampling's own error (`resample_roundtrip_control.txt`: a 1280 x 720 shot scaled up by the model, rounded to 8 bits and solved back): 71.5% of the pixels equal, 28.5% one level off, none more. Figures (`task6\shot_compare.py`, `final_compare.txt`; client area 921,600 pixels; outside what moves, which is every pixel that differs between two native shots of a run, grown by 8 px):

| Final build against | Over 1 level | Over 2 | Over 8 | Of those over 8: brighter / darker / mixed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the materials picture, bridge, 42 s and 74 s (run 304 against run 292 reconstructed) | 4.16% | 0.189% | **0.010%** (89 px) | 87 / 2 / 0 |
| the materials picture, third spot (run 306 at 50 s against run 275 at 176 s reconstructed; the camera is the same to the pixel) | 3.75% | 0.122% | **0.001%** (12 px) | 3 / 7 / 2 |
| the first build, 64-bit, bridge (run 294; the same against run 300) | 0.76% | 2 px | 0 (largest 3 levels) | - |
| the first build, 64-bit, third spot (run 296) | 0.59% | 3 px | 0 (largest 3 levels) | - |
| the experiment build with this format, bridge (run 299) | 0 | 0 | 0 (every pixel equal) | - |

So at the bridge and the third spot 0.010% and 0.001% of the pixels differ from the materials picture by more than 8 levels, everything counted; the criterion allows 1% outside terrain blend bands, and neither the bands nor the blended draws needed to be set apart. Where the bridge differs by more than 2 levels (1,745 pixels; `where_bridge_292r_299_over2.png` of the same picture in run 299, viewed): a patch of the dirt path at the left where the grass and dirt layers meet, thin lines on the ground beyond the rail and edges of the far bank, all brighter (the linear sums of terrain layers, as derived above, and far smaller in area than the arithmetic led me to expect), and the hero's hair, darker (blended cards on a moving figure at the edge of the moving mask). Against the 64-bit build the format's rounding is all there is: red and green 85% of the pixels equal and 15% one level off, blue 78% equal, 21% one off and 0.76% two off, the mean 0.15 and 0.23 of a level darker, no pixel outside what moves more than 3 levels off. *The trees have no such comparison.* In this display state the walk of the trees plan ends at another camera than in run 293 (52.6% of the pixels of the 64 s shots differ by more than 8 levels; `trees_third_alignment.txt`), and it does not end at exactly the same camera twice: runs 295 and 305 have the eye at (84.4618988, 171.927811, 49.7549744) and (84.4619598, 171.927811, 49.7550011) in their captures' frame rows, where the three bridge captures have one eye to the last digit. Run 305 against run 295 therefore differs by more than 8 levels in 0.82% of the pixels outside what moves, as single pixels scattered over the cut-out edges of leaves, ferns and grass with both signs (2,797 brighter, 3,025 darker, 1,745 mixed; `final_trees_where_over8.png`, viewed), which is the cameras and not the format; in the rest of the picture the two runs relate as at the bridge (three quarters of the pixels equal, a fifth to a quarter one level off). The trees against the materials picture are part of the pending check for the normal display state.

**In the game: the final build** (binary of 2026-10-08 02:40:00; runs 304 bridge, 305 trees, 306 third spot, 307 view cycle, 02:59 to 03:06, made by the controlling agent with `tools\drive_game.ps1 -UserPresent -BootSave Hero000`: verdict `RUN UNCHECKED: -UserPresent given, no idle gate and no watch`, exit 0, for all four; they are not gated runs, and they are in the fallback display state. Logs `task6f_bridge_fable_2_304.log`, `task6f_trees_fable_2_305.log`, `task6f_third_fable_2_306.log`, `task6f_cycle_fable_2_307.log`; captures `native_discovery_20261008_030024.jsonl` and `_030218`; my outputs `task6\final_checks_*.txt`, `final_compare.txt`, `final_cycle_307_checks.txt`.)

| Run | View off | Native | Split | Overlay |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bridge 304 | 35.1 ms (28.5) | 39.3, 37.2, 38.3 ms (25.5, 26.9, 26.1) | 38.4 (26.1) | 39.0 (25.7) |
| trees 305 | 33.3 ms (30.0) | 34.8, 34.5, 34.5, 34.6 ms (28.7 to 29.0) | 34.5 (29.0) | 35.2 (28.4) |
