Against the experiment runs at the bridge (the table further up: old-like pass 36.1 to 37.2 ms in the composite views with 33.8 ms view off; this format and step in run 299 35.8 to 37.6 with 34.1; the 64-bit target 38.4 to 39.4 with 34.1), run 304 reads 37.2 to 39.3 ms with 35.1 ms view off. Said plainly: **in absolute terms the final build is not within the old-like run's spread at the bridge** (four of its five composite windows are above that run's slowest), although it draws the same picture through the same GPU path as run 299 forty-four minutes earlier, which was (every pixel of the two runs' shots is equal). Its own view-off window is also 1.0 to 1.2 ms slower than the experiment runs' (35.1 against 33.8 to 34.4), so part of the difference is the session; counted from each run's own view-off frame the composite views cost 2.1 to 4.2 ms in run 304, 1.7 to 3.5 in run 299, 2.3 to 3.4 in the old-like run 301 and 4.3 to 5.3 and 3.2 to 4.2 in the two 64-bit runs: run 304 overlaps the old-like run and reaches 0.8 ms above it. And the first build's four runs (64-bit; 294 to 297, an hour earlier) read about what the final build's four read in the SDK's windows of the same plans (bridge 26.8 to 27.6 fps against 25.7 to 26.6; trees 29.1 to 29.7 against 28.8 to 29.5; third spot 24.3 to 24.7 against 24.7 to 25.3; cycle 26.1 to 26.3 and 22.7 against 26.1 to 26.7 and 22.7 to 24.0). So in this display state one configuration differs by 1.5 to 2 ms between two runs (64-bit: 294 and 300; this format: 299 and 304), which is as large as the differences between the formats that the six experiment runs showed: those runs favour the 32-bit target, these four do not show it, and **the acceptance "no slower than the old-like pass in the same display state" is neither shown nor refuted by single runs**. What would settle it is repeated runs in alternation, or the normal display state. A view-off stretch of these plans is one window (150 frames), too short for a control of its own; the heavier view after the cycle's turn reads 26.7 fps with the view off, so there the emulated frame alone is over the boundary.

**Pending for the normal display state** (the user's monitor on: shots of 1942 x 1136; listed in the task's report under "Pending game runs"): a renderer-off control (`-Total 70 -GameArgs "--fable2_native_render=false" -Env @{FABLE2_GUEST_WORK_LOG="1"}`) reading 30.0 fps as run 279 did; then one bridge and one trees run with the rules' plans: 30.0 fps in every native-view `frames:` window (success criterion 7), and the native shots at 42 / 74 s and 64 / 98 s against runs 292 / 293 pixel for pixel (success criterion 2 exactly, the trees included).

### Native light

Plan Task 7 (2026-10-08): the first lit pixels. With `--fable2_native_light=on`, now the default, the clay pass lights the terrain and the objects that carry a colour stream with the game's own ambient, baked and sun light, in linear space, without shadows. Code: `clay_logic.h` "Native light" (the pure mirrors `AmbientFactor`, `AmbientAxis`, `AmbientT`, `LightPow`, `FacingGate`, `SunGateSingle`, `LightTerrainLayer`, `LightTerrainFar`, `LightObject`, `ClayLit`; `ClayFrameConstants`, `ClayObjectConstants`, `SetClayTerrainLight`, `LightFamilyDrawn`, `LightVertexNeed`, `FormatLightDrawnText`; the light flag of `PlanTerrain`), `fable2_native_shaders.h` (the blocks `Frame` and `Object`, the light functions, the lit paths of the vertex shader and of three pixel shaders), `clay_pass.cpp` (`PrepareLight`, `PrepareTerrain`, `FinishTerrain`, `Render`), `geometry_cache.cpp` (`Normals`, `Colours`, `Indices`), `tools/xdk_sigmatch/light_check.py` (`--multi-sun`), `tools/xdk_sigmatch/terrain_replay.py` (`--lit`). Tests: `test_clay_logic.cpp` cases 420 to 484, `test_native_shaders.cpp` codes 31, 35 to 40, 45, 46, 64, 69 and 80 to 97, `tests/test_light_check.py` (`PinnedForTheRenderer`, `MultiSun`), `tests/test_terrain_replay.py` (the `lit` tests). Evidence: `task7\`.

**What is lit.** A drawn record is lit when the switch is on, its frame has a light block the pass can use, its pair of shaders has one of the families below, and everything the family reads is there:

| Family (`LightInputs`) | Pixel shaders | What the pass needs per draw | Function |
|---|---|---|---|
| `terrain-layer`, with its two- and four-light forms | `0x9C21...`, `0x4755...`; `0x4524...`, `0xAF1E...` (two lights), `0x04F2...`, `0x5783...` (four) | the vertex normals of its grid (tf17), the base map tf3 with `base_valid` inputs, `c11.x`, the facing literal 8 | `LightTerrainLayer` |
| `terrain-far` | `0x7DDA...`, `0x2F8A...` | the same without the literal | `LightTerrainFar` |
| `object` with vertex shader `0xECD6...` (`LightVertexKind::kStream`), with its two- and four-light forms | `0x8D90...`, `0x2FD2...`; `0x648B...` (two), `0x7892...` (four) | the stream normals and the colour stream's colours, the world rotation with a direction, `c27.w`, `c49.xyz`, `c26.xyz`, the power 2.2 | `LightObject` |
| `object` with probe colours (`kProbe`: vertex shaders `0x79EA...`, and the bone-skinned `0xD4D5...`, `0xA1F7...`), with its two- and four-light forms (plan Task 8a) | the same four | the normals (a skinned mesh's after its bones), the probe buffer (four weights and a set index per vertex), the twelve probe registers `c28..c39`, the world rotation with a direction, `c27.w`, `c49.xyz`, `c26.xyz` | `LightObject` with the probe colour |
| `tree-trunk` (`kProbe`: vertex shader `0x475E...`), with its two- and four-light forms (plan Task 8a) | `0x00E0...`; `0x58D3...` (two), `0x2FBE...` (four) | as the probe objects (the trunk's scale `c7.w`, 0.67 to 1.53, is inside the rows) | `LightObject` with the probe colour |

Everything else is drawn as the materials picture draws it, the unlit linear colour, and counted by reason (`ClayUnlit`): `light-off` (the switch, or the texture path off: the light is of the material), `light-block` (the frame has no block: every draw of it), `light-unknown` (no light inputs, a family the pass does not light yet: the plants and the leaves; a missing buffer, base map or value; a world rotation without a direction; a ground square unlit as a whole) and `normal-unsupported`. Nothing is lit from a guessed value: every number that reaches a shader is checked to be a number of usable size (`LightValueUsable`: magnitude at most 1,024; the captured values are below 2) by `MakeClayFrameConstants`, `MakeClayObjectConstants` and `SetClayTerrainLight`, each of which refuses as a whole.

**The two- and four-light forms** (controller amendment C2). These shaders compute light 0 in another form (`sun_gate_multi` of `lighting-formulas.md`) and the sun is not their light 0. Where it stands, from the rows' `"pfull"` of the two captures of plan Task 6's final runs (`task7\multi_slots.py`, `multi_slots_bridge_trees.txt`): in all 1,520 `-multi` main-scene rows of eight frames at the bridge and all 944 by the trees `c112` is one-hot, the light at its slot is the frame's sun (`xyz` bit-equal to `c109.xyz`, `w` = 0, `c94` = 0 there, direction and colour equal to the frame row's block in every frame that has one), and every other light with a colour is a lantern (`w` = 1, `c94` = 1). Every draw of a two-light shader has one lantern in slot 0 and the sun in slot 1; every draw of a four-light shader has two lanterns and the sun in slot 2. `light_check.py --multi-sun` then runs each pair's microcode with exactly that light set (the sun in the slot, the lanterns dark as spec ruling 5 has them, everything else of a trial random, the screen mask random too) against the family's ONE-light function fed that sun: all nine pairs of the terrain, object and trunk families agree in 400 of 400 trials at every slot behind light 0 (slot 1 for the five pairs of a two-light shader, slots 1 to 3 for the four pairs of a four-light one; largest relative difference 1.5e-15; `task7\light_check_multi_sun.txt`). The check fails when any one property of that light set is taken away (a lantern with its light, the mask on the lantern, range and cone on the sun, the sun as a position: `tests/test_light_check.py`, `MultiSun`). So the pairs in scope here, the four layer pairs and `0x648B...` / `0x7892...` with `0xECD6...`, are drawn with the frame's sun through the one-light function, without their lanterns; the trunks' two pairs passed the same check for plan Task 8.

**As drawn.** The HLSL functions are those of `tools/xdk_sigmatch/light_formulas.py` statement for statement and carry its names and argument lists; the C++ mirrors in `clay_logic.h` are pinned to numbers computed by the Python functions, the same literals in `tests/test_light_check.py` (`PinnedForTheRenderer`) and `test_clay_logic.cpp` (cases 420 to 431).

- *Terrain, vertex shader.* The normal is the game's: `(x, y)` from the record's buffer (t8), `z = sqrt(|1 - x^2 - y^2|)`, not normalised. For a lit record the vector of the ambient factor is made per vertex as the game makes it, `axis = lerp(n, C, (1 + n . C) / 2)` with `C` the block's `axis` (pixel `c23.xyz`, equal to vertex `c19.xyz`), and interpolated.
- *Terrain layer, pixel shader.* `N = normalize(n)`, `t = sat(normalize(axis) . N)`, `L = c22 + (1 - c22) t^2`; `colour = A * c11.x * (pow(tf3, c21.w) * c21.xyz * L + c20.xyz) + A * c87.xyz * sat(N . sun) * sat(8 * (N . sun)) * 1`, `A` the layer's linear colour (`tf0^2` or the steep-ground mix), tf3 the base map at the base coordinate, the last 1 the screen mask. The layer's alpha (weight times fade) is not lit.
- *Far terrain.* `t = sat(N . c23.xyz)` with the constant as it is, `colour = F * (pow(tf3, c21.w) * c21.xyz * L + c20.xyz) + F * c11.x * c87.xyz * sat(N . sun)`; the underlay `0x2F8A...` then multiplies by its fade.
- *Object, vertex shader.* `o1 = M n` with `M = world_rot * world_scale` (the game's `(R n) * c7.w`; the scalar is inside the rows the shader gets, so its sign counts), not normalised; `axis = lerp(o1, C, (1 + o1 . C) / 2)`; the colour is the stream's red, green and blue (bytes 0 to 2 of the cache's entry) over 255, raised to the draw's power 2.2. A draw whose `M` has no direction (`ClassifyRotation`: degenerate) is not lit; mirrored and skewed ones are lit with the game's own `M n` (ruling of 2026-10-08), and the classes are still counted in the `Light inputs:` line.
- *Object, pixel shader.* `colour = (A * c27.w * (pow(vcolour, c21.w) * c21.xyz * L + c20.xyz) + A * c87.xyz * sat(N . sun) * 1) * c49.xyz`, then times `c26.xyz`. `A` is the albedo squared, after the division by its alpha where the shader has it and clamped to [0, 1] before the square, as the materials picture clamps it (the game does not clamp there).
- *A zero normal* is never made a direction: `light_normalize` gives zero for a vector without a length, so `N . sun` and `t` are 0: no sun and the ambient at its floor, which is what the game's saturating dot products give. The power of a zero base is 0 by a select, and nothing is divided.
- *The sun* is one vector for `c80.xyz` and `c109.xyz` (`ClayFrameConstants::sun_dir`): the block is valid only where the two are bit-equal.
- *Blue has no ambient floor*: the block carries `c22.xy`, and `c22.z` is taken as 0, which it is in every captured row.

**Scale.** The value written is the formula's value without the game's output scale `c20.w` (16): the game multiplies every scene shader's colour by it and its later passes take it back; here the output mapping's exposure stands in its place. `c49.xyz` is inside the object's function. `c26.xyz`, which the game applies together with `c20.w` after its fog, multiplies the function's result. Both are (1, 1, 1) in every captured row.

**The exposure is not calibrated.** `ClayExposure(on)` is 1; plan Task 9 fits it. There is one exposure and one place it comes from: `ClayFrameExposure`, which is `ClayExposure(on)` for a lit frame and `ClayExposure(off)` = 1 for a frame drawn without light, into root-constant word 0 of the mapping's own block. The frame's block has no exposure field. Once `E` is not 1 the clear colour of a lit frame will map to `sqrt(E)` times its levels unless the clear value is divided by `E` (comment at `kClayClearLinear`), and an unlit draw inside a lit frame will be scaled by `E` with everything else: both are Task 9's to decide.

**Where the values reach the shaders.**

- *The frame's block*: cbuffer `Frame` at register b2 (`ClayFrameConstants`, 96 bytes: sun direction, sun colour, tint, exponent, added light, floor, axis, and `light_on`), in slot 0 of the frame's constant buffer, bound once per `Render` for the vertex and pixel stages. It is made from the scene's own block and from nothing else, so a frame is lit by its own light or not at all.
- *Per draw, in the root constants* (cbuffer `Draw`, 144 bytes as before): two of the three free words are `light_mode` (bit 0: the vertex shader forms the terrain normal from t8; bit 1: it reads an object's normal at t9 and its word at t10 and the rows of `Object`; bit 3, with bit 1: the word is the vertex's probe set and the colour is the probe colour, with the weights at t11 and the probe registers of `Object`; bit 2: the draw is lit) and `light_count` (elements the light's buffers hold: a buffer is read only for a vertex below it). One word stays free.
- *Per draw, in a constant buffer slot at register b1*: the root signature has 64 words and holds 56 now (36 of constants, 2 for each of seven buffers and two constant buffers, 1 for each of two texture tables; 54 before plan Task 8a added t11), so the object's nine matrix values, `c27.w`, `c49`, `c26` and the power (17 values) do not fit the root constants. They are cbuffer `Object` (`ClayObjectConstants`: 80 bytes, and since plan Task 8a 272 with the twelve probe registers, in a slot of 512 bytes: "Probe colours" below) in a slot of the constant buffer that already held one 256-byte slot per terrain record; a lit terrain record's values (`t_base_uv`, `t_base_sampler`, `t_c11x`, `t_facing_scale`) are appended to its cbuffer `Terrain` (`ClayTerrainConstants`, 192 bytes, was 160). The buffer is one per frame, pooled as before: slot 0 the frame, then the terrain records, then the lit objects. It is taken for every frame that draws anything (before: only for a frame with terrain records), so that the two registers the vertex shader declares, b1 and b2, are bound at every draw whether or not the draw reads them. Without it (an allocation that fails) the frame has no terrain material, as before, and no light.
- *Buffers and textures*: t8 the terrain normals (float2; until now the steep-ground block read them through the UV register t2, which holds UVs only again), t9 an object's normals (float3), t10 its colours (uint; for a probe object the set index of each vertex), t11 a probe object's weights (float4; plan Task 8a); t7, the fourth entry of the terrain texture table, the base map. **Every draw binds all of them** (t8, t9, t10, and t11 since plan Task 8a; the sentences that follow were written for the first three and hold for the four): its own buffer where its `light_mode` names it, else its own positions (as t2 for a draw without UVs), and the vertex shader loads from them only inside `[branch]` blocks on `light_mode` and `light_count`. Both are needed and both are tested: a buffer bound by its address (a root descriptor) has no bounds check on the GPU, and without the attribute the compiler turns the `if` into a select and loads all three for every vertex of every draw, at that draw's vertex indices, from whatever an earlier draw left bound (the first build did exactly that and its three lit runs ended in a removed device; that this was the cause is likely and not proven by an A/B, see "In the game"). The positions hold 16 bytes for every vertex a draw can name, more than any of the three elements (12, 8, 4), so even an unguarded load would lie inside them. The binding layout is made from one table, `kClayBindings` (`clay_logic.h`: kind, register, count, stage for the root parameters: eleven, twelve with t11, which is the backend's limit), and the shader tests hold every resource each compiled shader declares against that table for its stage, that no shader has a loop instruction, that the three loads are inside a branch while t0, t1 and t2 are not, and that no pixel shader loads from a buffer (codes 150 to 159, each with a control that fails). Which of the three is a draw's own is one pure rule, `LightBufferSources(light_mode)` (cases 485 to 488), and the pass binds the three in one loop over it. Two rules of the draw path were tightened in fix round 1, both for the same reason (nothing the shader is given may be unbound or out of range). *The index rule has a low end*: a record is drawn only if every vertex its triangles name lies inside the positions, `min_index + base_vertex >= 0` as well as `max_index + base_vertex < count` (`IndexRangeValid`; the index entry keeps the smallest index of its list; a record failing it is skipped as `bad-index`). Until then the low end was left to the vertex shader's own check, which compiles to a select after the load, so a negative base vertex with an index below it would have loaded from before the start of the positions and UVs (the review counted 0 of 10,236 captured rows with a negative base vertex; not recounted here). *A frame without its constant buffer draws nothing*: b1 and b2 are views of the frame's constant buffer, and when that buffer cannot be made (an allocation failure) no record of the frame is drawn (`ClayRecordsWithoutConstants`; counted under `render-other`, logged once); the targets are still cleared and mapped, so the native view shows the clear colour for that frame and never a stale picture. Until then such a frame drew its records flat with b1 and b2 unset. A draw without light also reads no light input, in either stage. That is tested on the program such a draw runs: each shader is compiled once more with `light_mode` a constant (0: no light, no steep ground; 1: steep ground without light), and what the compiler leaves must read neither cbuffer `Frame` nor (outside the terrain shaders, whose b1 is the terrain block) `Object`, load from no light buffer the mode does not name, and not sample the base map; the lit constants (6, 5) must read them, and two controls (an unlit colour and a vertex output that take a value from `Frame`) must be reported (codes 160 to 162). So what an unlit draw shows cannot depend on what b1, b2, t7, t9 and t10 hold, on any adapter. And the light paths themselves are pinned as text, because tests that look for single statements cannot see one that is added between them. Since plan Task 8a the pins are six pieces that leave no statement between a shader's inputs and its lit output outside them: the shared light block (cbuffer `Frame` and every light function, `light_normalize` to `clay_lit`, in each of the four shaders that light), the vertex shader's whole `main`, the textured pixel shader from `clay_linear` to the end of `main`, the terrain shaders' common functions (`sample_colour`, `sample_base`, `terrain_fade_in`, `terrain_out`), the layer shader from `sample_side` to the end of `main`, and the far shader's whole `main` (codes 163 to 165). Outside them are the declarations, which reflection holds against their tables and the layout, and `alpha_passes`. (As first written, in fix round 1 of plan Task 7, the pins were the five lit blocks alone, and the re-review showed that the same kind of change placed one line outside a block passed: a statement inside `LightObject`, `LightTerrainLayer`, `light_sun`, `AmbientAxis` or `sample_base`, the layer's normal negated or the far colour squared above the lit branch, the vertex colour halved above the textured shader's branch, the far shader's base coordinate replaced. Those nine are controls now, with the first review's five and with an insertion, a deletion and two reorderings inside the probe colour's block: 25 changes, each of which compiles and must be reported by the piece it is in.) Every reflection test holds a block's compiled layout against a table beside the HLSL and the table against the C++ struct, field by field (`kClayFrameFields`, `kClayObjectFields`, `kClayTerrainFields`; codes 88 to 97, cases 440 to 464).

**Probe colours** (plan Task 8a, 2026-10-08; in the game: the end of this paragraph). The objects without a colour stream and the trunks take their baked term from a colour the game's vertex shader makes per vertex: `o6 = c[28 + a0].xyz + (c[29 + a0] . w, c[30 + a0] . w, c[31 + a0] . w)`, four-component dot products, `w` the vertex's four weights and `a0` (0, 4 or 8) the set its position names (`lighting-formulas.md`, "Objects", `probe_colour`). *The power it takes*: none in the vertex shader (the colour stream's 2.2 is `0xECD6...`'s own literal); the pixel shader's `pow(x, c21.w)` is the only one, and the game computes it as `exp2(c21.w * log2(|x|))`: the logarithm's operand is the absolute value in all seven pixel shaders (`log r6.x, r_abs[6].x` in `0x8D90...`, instructions 37 to 39; `r_abs[6]` in `0x2FD2...`, `0x648B...`, `0x7892...`; `r_abs[8]` in the trunks' `0x00E0...`, `0x58D3...`, `0x2FBE...`). So a colour that the signed weights make exactly zero has no baked term (the added light and the sun remain), and a negative one is lit like its mirror image: no clamp at zero, no `max`, no NaN. `light_pow` and `LightPow` are that rule, and the mirrors are pinned to `light_formulas.py` on 0, -1e-8, -0.06 and on a probe set that gives exactly 0 and -0.0625 (cases 490 to 494; `PinnedForTheRenderer`, `test_probe_object`, `test_probe_colour_zero_and_negative`). *As drawn*: the clay vertex shader, under `light_mode` bit 3, reads the vertex's word at t10 as the set index, clamps it to 8 (`min(word, 8u)`; the buffer holds 0, 4 or 8 only, and the clamp keeps any other word inside the twelve registers), reads the weights at t11 and forms the colour from `o_probe[a0]` to `o_probe[a0 + 3]` of cbuffer `Object`; the pixel shader is the colour-stream objects' program unchanged (compiled with the mode fixed at 14 and at 6 the two listings are identical). A bone-skinned mesh's normals are in the cache after its bones (`normalize(B3 n)`) and go through the draw's rows `M` like any other object's. *Registers*: t10 is one `uint` per vertex whichever kind it is; for a probe object the pass binds the probe buffer there from `ProbeSetOffset(probe_count)`, the buffer's second region (a multiple of 256 bytes: the first buffer the pass binds from an offset that is not 0), and the same buffer from byte 0 at t11, the twelfth and last binding parameter. `LightBufferSources` names the four sources per `light_mode` and `LightBufferOffset` the offset; the count is the one the probe buffer was built with, never the draw's `light_count`, which is the smaller of that and the normals' count and bounds every load. *Constants*: `ClayObjectConstants` is 272 bytes with the registers, so a lit object's slot is 512 bytes (`kClayObjectSlotBytes`); the small slots (the frame's, the terrain's) stay 256 and the objects' follow them (`ClayObjectSlotOffset`). No view has a size, and the vertex shader declares the 272-byte block for every draw, so the buffer holds 512 bytes from the start of every slot the pass binds: without objects the last small slot is padded (`ClayConstantBufferBytes`; case 499, with the count at which the unpadded size ends exactly on a 64 KB step). `MakeClayObjectConstants` refuses the whole block when one of the forty-eight probe components, or any value the colour-stream block was refused for, is not a number of magnitude at most 1,024 (cases 495 to 497), and a record is lit only with the second buffer of its own vertex kind (`LightObjectReady`: an instanced record's colours light nothing; case 498). *The compiled vertex shader, read* (`task8a\disasm\kClayVs.asm`; vs_5_0, the runtime's flags): seven structured-buffer loads. t1, t0 and t2 (the indices, the positions, the UVs) are outside every block, as before. t8 is inside `if_nz` of `(light_mode & 1) && in range && v < light_count`. t9 and t10 are inside `if_nz` of `(light_mode & 2) &&` the same bound. t11 is inside that block and inside a second `if_nz` of `light_mode & 8`, after `umin` of the word with 8; the four constant-buffer reads there are `cb1[r + 5]` to `cb1[r + 8]` with `r` at most 8, inside the seventeen registers the shader declares (`CB1[17], dynamicIndexed`). No `else` side holds a load. The colour stream's power is read in the `else` of the inner block (`cb1[4].w`). Compiled once for each of the sixteen values of `light_mode`, the program loads from exactly the light registers at which `LightBufferSources` has the draw's own buffer (and not from t8 where the object bit is set too, a mode the pass never makes), declares no register of `Object` without the object bit, five (80 bytes, fixed index) with it and all seventeen only with the probe bit, and has the `umin` only with the probe bit (code 160; `task8a\disasm_summary.txt`). No pixel shader loads from a buffer, and the textured one declares five registers of `Object`. *What the attribute does here*: with the text of plan Task 7, taking the two `[branch]` attributes away made the compiler load from t8, t9 and t10 outside every block, the form of the three hung runs; with the present text it keeps every light load inside its test with or without the attributes (`task8a\flat_control.txt`). The attribute stays as the promise in the source, the compiled program is what is held, and the test's controls now put a load outside its test by hand (code 158; one of them, a load as a vertex output's default, the compiler moves into the `else` of the object's test, where a count of blocks does not see it and the program of a draw without light does: the reason the check is made per `light_mode`). *The pairs*: `light_check.py` was run on five pairs that were not in the table, `0x2FD2...` with `0xECD6...` and `0x648B...` / `0x7892...` with the skinned `0xD4D5...` / `0xA1F7...` (400 of 400 trials each, largest relative difference 8.9e-16; with `--multi-sun` every two- and four-light pair of the terrain, object and trunk families, thirteen now, is its one-light function at every slot behind light 0: `task8a\light_check_object.txt`, `light_check_object_multi.txt`, `light_check_multi_sun.txt`); they are entered. Of the five only `0x648B...` with `0xA1F7...` is in a capture so far (2 draws a frame at the third spot). *The debug layer's messages*: `--fable2_native_d3d_messages=true` (off by default) writes the Direct3D debug layer's errors, corruption and warnings to the game log as `[native] d3d ERROR id N (seen k): text` (at most 4 lines per severity and id, 400 in a run) and a count line with every 300th clay frame's lines, `[native] d3d messages: errors E, warnings W`, so that a run under `--d3d12_debug=true` shows a rejected call, or that there was none, without a debugger (`D3dMessageLog`, `render/d3d_messages.cpp`; plan Task 7 had a temporary form of it). *In the game* (runs 324 to 328 of 2026-10-08, made by the controlling agent with the user at the machine, `RUN UNCHECKED`; logs and shots `task8a_*`, the analysis in `task8a\`: `runs_summary_task8a.txt`, `pictures.txt`, `figure_colours.txt`, `third_hero.txt`, `same_programs.txt`). On the software adapter with the debug layer and the message switch (run 324; the user closed the window at 251 s, so there is no shot): the layer's queue was available, the world ran lit for about two minutes (`lit 738 (terrain-layer 492, terrain-far 72, object 144, tree-trunk 30)`), and the layer reported no error and no corruption: `d3d messages: errors 0, warnings 14`, later `15`, all of the two ids plan Task 7's run on that adapter had (1328, a buffer's initial state ignored at its creation; 1008, one resource in two barrier descriptions), neither of which names a root parameter, a view or a shader. So t10 bound from the probe buffer's offset is accepted as it is; the fallback (a buffer of its own for the set indices) was not needed and is not built. On the GPU (runs 325 bridge, 326 third spot, 327 trees, 328 bridge with the light off): no device removal, no skipped record, `Light inputs: ready N of N` with every world rotation a rotation and no refused probe buffer. Lit share of the drawn records: bridge 738 of 921 to 923 (80%; 74% before this task), third spot 766 of 977 to 979 (78%; 70%), trees 721 or 722 of 863 to 866 (83% to 84%; 66%); objects 144 / 161 / 207 or 208 (120 / 133 / 120 before) and trunks 30 / 49 / 68 (none before). The pixel shaders of the object and trunk families are gone from the `unknown` list; what it holds now is the plants (`0xA7E4...`, `0x165E...`, `0xE99F...`, `0x2605...`, `0xF525...`), the leaves (`0x7CD5...`), the blended characters (`0x401A...`) and the water. The pictures. The window of these runs was 1920 x 1080 where every baseline run's was 1280 x 720, so nothing could be compared pixel for pixel: the comparisons are of pictures brought to 1280 x 720, by region means. *What was lit before did not change*: at the bridge and the third spot the regions of plan Task 7 (planks, dirt, hills, far hill, lake, grass, stockade) read 0.997 to 1.005 of their luminance in runs 322 and 315, the same 1.001 to 1.004 the light-off pair of runs reads against run 323 (the resampling's own bias); and compiled with `light_mode` fixed at 0, 1, 5 and 6, each of the five clay shaders is instruction for instruction the program it was before this task (20 of 20 listings identical), so an unlit draw, lit ground and a lit colour-stream object run the code they ran. At the trees the camera stood about 6 pixels from where run 316's stood (the stick plan under another frame pacing) and the posts' fixed rectangles read 3% to 6% lower: that comparison is not one of like with like, and the trees rest on the identical programs alone. *Light off* (run 328 against run 323): region means 1.001 to 1.004, mean difference outside what moves 1.4 levels where resampling one picture two ways gives 1.0; that it is pixel-equal, as it was in every earlier task, could not be shown with a window of another size. *The newly lit*, against the emulated shot taken four seconds before each lit one. The trunk at the bridge: 1.72 times the planks' luminance while it was unlit, 0.90 lit, 0.99 in the emulated frame; its colour went from pale beige (red : green : blue 1.06 : 1 : 0.78) to the emulated frame's dark grey-green (0.78 : 1 : 0.86 against 0.77 : 1 : 0.81); the trunks by the trees likewise, dark where they were the brightest things in the picture. The hero: at the bridge, in full sun, about as bright as when unlit (shirt 1.52 times the planks against 1.56; emulated 1.85) with the hue now the emulated one (0.89 : 1 : 0.85 against 0.88 : 1 : 0.85; unlit it was 0.89 : 1 : 1.03, too blue); from the front at the third spot his face reads 1.39 times the rail in front of him (1.63 unlit, 1.54 emulated), his trousers 1.01 (1.27; 0.95), the vest 0.81 (0.84; 0.70), the dog's chest 1.69 (2.04; 1.75). No figure, trunk or prop is black, white or of a wrong colour in the three lit shots viewed with their crops: the zero and negative probe colours leave no mark that can be seen. What stays wrong beside them: no shadows (the hero under the trees gets the sun the canopy would take), the plants and leaves unlit and brighter than what is lit, the dog without its fur shells, and the whole lit picture darker than the emulated one (the exposure is 1). *Cost*, within these runs: 30.0 fps in every view at all three spots (frame medians 32.9 to 34.3 ms, the display's pacing; the baseline runs were made in another display state and are not comparable); the clay pass's largest total over 300 frames, once a spot has loaded, 3.0 to 5.4 ms at the bridge, 4.5 to 6.5 at the third spot, 4.7 to 5.0 by the trees, none above its baseline window; the frame's constant buffer 262,144 bytes at all three spots (565 small slots and 174 object slots at the bridge; 196,608 before, with 685 slots of 256 bytes); geometry resident 39.3 MB at the bridge (35.9 in run 322, and 39.3 in the light-off run of this build too; the third spot's and the trees' runs read 36.4 and 36.5 MB at the same place in their first window, so it is no cost of this task and it is not explained), 45.9 MB at the third spot (45.7 after plan Task 5b), 76.1 MB by the trees (76.8). The shader test's executable is built as `native_shaders_task8a.exe` on the development machine until a file its antivirus restored is deleted (`tests\run_native_tests.cmd` itself is unchanged).

**The base map.** tf3, the region's baked light (one 768 x 768 DXT1 texture for all 492 layer records and 59 of the 75 far records of a bridge frame, four smaller ones for the other far records; clamped, linear filter), is resolved through the texture cache like the other terrain textures, only in a lit frame and only for a record whose light inputs, normals and `base_valid` inputs are there. Its coordinate is `world.xy * base_uv.xy + base_uv.zw` from the inputs kept since the materials sub-project, scaled into the host texture; its sampler is the one its fetch constant asks for; it is fetched with explicit derivatives, formed outside the light's branch. A record whose base map is not usable this frame (not resolved, over the upload budget, a format the cache does not decode) is not lit. **A ground square is lit as a whole or not at all**: `TerrainPlanItem` carries a light flag, in "can be lit", out "is lit", and `PlanTerrain`, after it has decided which records keep their material, marks a square unlit when one layer drawn with its material in it cannot be lit, ties squares through a layer draw of several patches as it does for the material, and takes the light from every layer of an unlit square. That was added to the plan's own pass rather than made a second fallback of the material: an unlit square keeps its material. A draw of the adaptive builder is lit or not by itself; where a lit underlay fades into an unlit square the two differ across the ring of the cross-fade (counted as `light-unknown`, not hidden).

**What an unlit draw looks like beside lit ones.** The unlit linear colour of the materials picture, unchanged: the albedo with the facet shade, squared. The spec's table had "the frame's ambient with the baked term 1 and L = 1" for such draws, `A * (c21.xyz + c20.xyz)`. Measured with the block of the bridge capture (`task7\lit_object_survey.py`, `lit_object_survey_bridge.txt`: the object function with `A` = 1 on every vertex of the 120 colour-stream object draws of a frame, 295,717 vertices, not weighted by area): the mean light factor of a lit object is (0.343, 0.401, 0.345), luminance 0.385, of which the sun is 0.209 (half of the vertices face it); the plain albedo is the factor 1, 2.6 times that; the frame ambient with baked term 1 is (1.131, 1.459, 1.982), luminance 1.43, 3.7 times that and blue. Real baked terms are far below 1, so that form is the brightest ambient there could be. For the ground (`terrain_lit_unlit_means.txt`, four replayed squares): lit against unlit luminance 1.20 and 1.00 on the two flat squares in the sun, 0.81 and 0.70 on the two that hold a bank facing away from it. So the plain albedo is the less conspicuous of the two: as bright as lit ground in the sun, and brighter than a lit object by 1.6 times as displayed. It is still visible, and it will be until the families of plan Tasks 5b and 8 are lit.

**The facet shade** is a display-space factor and stays inside the square for an unlit draw. A lit draw has none: its light is the game's, and the shade would shade it a second time. The light multiplies the linear colour, outside the square (`LightObject(clay_linear(albedo), ...)`, never `clay_linear(albedo * light)`).

**What a lit draw may write.** Lit values exceed 1 (a lit value is its albedo squared times up to about 2.4 with the captured block), so the clamp at 1 is gone for them and the mapping clamps after the exposure. What is kept is `clay_lit`: a NaN and a negative value give 0 by a comparison and a mask, and the value is at most 64 (`kClayLitMax`). The bound is what keeps an infinity out of the target, from which a blend factor of zero would make a NaN that stays: 1,008 blended draws of 64 over one pixel are still below 64,512, the largest value of the target's 10-bit channel, and a frame has about 500 blended draws in all. The compiled shaders keep the comparison, the mask and the bound (code 69). An unlit draw keeps its clamps to [0, 1] exactly as they were, and the lit branch is a real branch on `light_mode`, so with the light off the shaders execute the statements of the linear-target build. Alpha is never lit, never squared, and clamped only where it is written.

**Rounding.** The target is `R11G11B10_FLOAT`, and this GPU rounds toward zero when it stores (review of plan Task 6): every blend loses up to 1/64 of the value in red and green and 1/32 in blue, always downward. A ground square is the sum of about five blended layers, so a lit square reads darker than the formula's sum by up to a few such steps; a comparison of a native shot with the replay has to allow for it (the replay computes in double precision).

**Smaller things changed with it.** (1) The light buffers of a mesh record have to hold the vertices its indices name, its largest vertex plus one (`LightVertexNeed`, the capture's own rule for the streams), no longer every vertex of the position stream: meshes share streams, and a colour stream ends where its last mesh ends. (2) A terrain record's light inputs are counted (`Light inputs: ready R of L`) after the plan, when it is known that the record keeps its material. (3) The comment on `kMirrored`: for an orthonormal matrix the inverse transpose is the matrix, sign included.

**The Light line** (F3's sixth line; `[native] clay: Light: ...` every 300 frames): `Light: on, block valid, lit N (terrain-layer a, terrain-far b, object c, tree-trunk d, plant e, plant-lit f), unlit U {light-unknown: u, normal-unsupported: v}, unknown {0x<pixel shader>: n, ...}, sun (x, y, z) colour (r, g, b)`; `block missing` and `unlit U {light-block: U}` for a frame without a block; `Light: off` with `unlit U {light-off: U}`.

**Checked without the game.**

- *The replay.* `terrain_replay.py --lit` replays a ground square as the pass lights it, from the capture's frame row (`"light"`) and the rows' `"lin"`, with the normals read at the grid points of each draw's own patch. Four squares at the dirt patch left of the bridge, frame 2 of `native_discovery_20261008_030024.jsonl` (`task7\replay_bridge_f2_sq30_sq48_unlit_lit_x4.png`, `..._sq31_sq49_...`, viewed): the lit squares keep the materials' pattern; flat ground in the sun is a little brighter and warmer than the unlit replay (the dirt of square 30: 1.13, 1.21, 1.15 times in linear red, green, blue); the bank that falls to the lake in squares 31 and 49 faces away from the sun (`N . sun` from -0.18 to -0.45 at the points printed) and is dark blue-grey, the ambient alone.
- *Against the emulated frame* (`task7\dirt_rect.py`, `dirt_rect.txt`; rectangles picked by eye; the emulated shot is `task6f_bridge_shot_36s.png`): the clear tan dirt of the emulated frame reads (168.5, 140.7, 85.8) of 255, the lit replay's dirt (112.8, 95.7, 60.6): a ratio of 1.49, 1.47, 1.42 as displayed and 2.21, 2.12, 1.94 in the squares of the display values. The three channels agree to within 5% of each other, where the unlit replay's ratios are 1.58, 1.62, 1.51. No threshold was set for this task; the number is what the exposure will have to cover, with whatever the game's later passes add. The square's whole outline in the shot could not be used: it holds the bridge's posts (`replay_against_shot.py`, `replay_against_emulated_bridge_sq30_sq48.txt`), and squares 31 and 49 lie under the bridge's planks.

**In the game** (before the task's own runs). The first build (2026-10-08 04:23) was run four times by the controlling agent. Light off (run 311, bridge): clean for 80 s, `Light: off, lit 0 (...), unlit 923 {light-off: 923}`, and its native shots at 42 s and 74 s against run 304's (`task6f_bridge_shot_42s.png`, `_74s.png`) with Task 6's `shot_compare.py`: **0 pixels differ outside what moves** in either pair (what moves: 1.71% of the client area; `task7\off_compare_bridge_311_304.txt`). Light on (runs 308 bridge, 309 third spot, 310 trees): each ended at 29 to 30 s with `D3D12 device removed: HRESULT 0x887A0006 - DEVICE_HUNG`, 4.9 s after the log line of the first frame of the world (`textures: burst frame 1`), which is the first frame with a light block; the menu's frames before it (`Light: on, block missing, lit 0`) were clean. By reading: no clay shader has a loop or a call, the per-draw words and the two constant buffers are set for every draw, the constant buffer's slots lie inside it; the one defect found is the unguarded loads through stale bindings described under "Buffers and textures" above, which the light-off run also made (from other buffers) without harm, so by reading it was a candidate and not a proven cause (`task7\stale_buffer_reads_bridge.txt`).

The build with that defect fixed (05:04, with temporary diagnostics since removed) was run twice by the controlling agent, both with the SDK's `--d3d12_debug=true` (`RUN UNCHECKED`; `task7_diag_D0_*`, `task7_diag_D1_*`). On the software adapter (`Microsoft Basic Render Driver`, 300 s, about 2.5 s a frame) and on the GPU (120 s, 18.8 fps under the debug layer) it ran to the end: no device removal, no error or corruption message of the debug layer (warnings of two kinds, both first seen about 20 s before the first lit frame: id 1328, a buffer's initial state ignored at its creation, and id 1008, one resource named in two barrier descriptions of a call; the temporary dump logged the first four of each), and `Light: on, block valid, lit 684 (terrain-layer 492, terrain-far 72, object 120, tree-trunk 0, plant 0, plant-lit 0), unlit 231 to 242 {light-unknown: ...}` in every line after the world's first four frames (those are the frames in which the scene comes in: lit 106 to 684, one of them the ground alone). **The cause of the three removed devices is recorded as: the stale unbounded loads of t8 / t9 / t10; likely, not proven by an A/B.** The build that would have reproduced the hang with the old bindings was not run (the controller's ruling), so nothing shows that the old rule hangs under otherwise equal conditions; what is shown is that the build without it does not.

*The red dog on the software adapter* (`task7\warp_dog.py`, `warp_dog.txt`, `warp_dog_x4.png`). In that run's one shot (280 s, native view) the dog is a flat red silhouette; in the GPU run's shots it is the dark textured dog of every earlier native shot. Measured: 5,124 pixels, red 231 to 238 of 255 for nine tenths of them (median 235), green and blue exactly 0 in 96% (the rest is the edge). The dog's body is `0xD4D5...` with pixel shader `0x8D90...`: not lit in this task (it has no colour stream: counted under `light-unknown`, 20 draws of that pixel shader in both runs), so it goes through the unlit textured statement, `display = t.rgb * (0.75 + 0.25 * facet)`; for a texel of exactly (1, 0, 0) and the flat screen normal of a small near object that is (235.7, 0, 0). So the pixel shader ran its ordinary unlit path and the texture gave pure red: no light input is involved (codes 160 to 162), its t8 / t9 / t10 hold its own positions and are not loaded from (codes 155, 156; case 485), and the same statement draws the hero, who looks the same in both runs (0.07% of his box differs by more than 32 levels, the sun of the two runs being a different one). What differs between the adapters is therefore the texture's content in guest memory, and that fits what the texture is: the dog's 512 x 512 texture (`0x15270000`, DXT1) is not a file image, the game renders the dog's and the hero's skin on the GPU and the emulator reads the result back into guest memory (`plans\hero-dog-texture-readback.md`; without the readback both are black, and a readback of the wrong moment gave "the red / constantly-shifting dog" there, in the emulated picture, long before this renderer). It is the one input of the native picture that is produced by the adapter and not by the game's CPU code. **Inference, not verified**: the run has no emulated shot, so it is not shown that the emulated dog was red too. It would be shown by one more run on the software adapter with a shot in each view (the task's report has the line); nothing in the native pass was changed for it.

**In the game: the lit picture** (binary of 2026-10-08 05:37:53, the fix without diagnostics; runs 314 bridge, 315 third spot, 316 trees with the light on and 317 bridge with `--fable2_native_light=off`, 05:50 to 05:58, made by the controlling agent with `tools\drive_game.ps1 -UserPresent -BootSave Hero000`: verdict `RUN UNCHECKED`, exit 0 each, no device removal; the display is the 1024 x 768 fallback, shots 1296 x 759; `task7r_<spot>_*`, scripts and outputs in `task7\`). Each lit run has an emulated shot 4 s before and 4 s after its first native shot, then split, overlay, native, and a capture in the native view.

- *Light off is the Task 6 picture.* Run 317's native shots at 42 s and 74 s against run 304's with Task 6's `shot_compare.py` (`off_compare_bridge_317_304.txt`): **0 pixels differ outside what moves** in either pair (2,347 and 2,346 pixels differ in all, every one of them inside what moves, which is 1.68% of the client area); `Light: off, lit 0 (...), unlit 921 {light-off: 921}`, Terrain 564 of 564. (Run 311 of the first build gave the same 0.)
- *What is lit* (the last `Light:` line of each run; `block valid` in every line after the menu, no `light-block`):

  | spot | lit | terrain-layer | terrain-far | object | unlit (`light-unknown`) | lit share of the drawn | terrain with material | sun direction, colour |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | bridge | 684 | 492 | 72 | 120 | 237 | 74% | 564 of 564 | (-0.596, -0.011, 0.803), (0.977, 0.817, 0.393) |
  | third spot | 689 | 481 | 75 | 133 | 288 | 71% | 556 of 556 | (-0.562, -0.045, 0.826), (0.966, 0.806, 0.387) |
  | trees | 566 | 426 | 20 | 120 | 298 | 66% | 446 of 446 | (-0.524, -0.079, 0.848), (0.953, 0.794, 0.380) |

  Every terrain record is lit (the lit layer and far counts add up to the records drawn with their material at each spot). The unlit draws by pixel shader, largest first: bridge `0xA7E4...` 60, `0x165E...` 39, `0x00E0...` 30, `0x7CD5...` 22, `0x8D90...` 20, `0xE99F...` 15, `0x401A...` 14, `0xF6D9...` 12; third spot `0xA7E4...` 53, `0x165E...` 47, `0x00E0...` 37, `0x7CD5...` 35, `0xE99F...` 28, `0x8D90...` 22, `0xF525...` 18, `0x58D3...` 12; trees `0x8D90...` 73, `0x00E0...` 40, `0x7CD5...` 40, `0xA7E4...` 29, `0x165E...` 23, `0x401A...` 22, `0x58D3...` 17, `0x648B...` 13. They are the families outside this task (plants, leaves, trunks, characters) and the draws of an object pixel shader on a mesh without a colour stream (`0x8D90...` and `0x648B...` with skinned or instanced vertex shaders: the hero, the dog, and by the trees 73 draws of `0x8D90...`). `Light inputs: ready R of R` in every line of the three runs, world rotation `rotation` for all.
- *Bridge* (`task7r_bridge_shot_40s.png` against the emulated `36s` and `44s` and the unlit `task7r_bridge_off_shot_42s.png`; sheets `real_bridge_planks.png`, `_hills.png`, `_bank.png`, `_rock.png`: emulated, lit, unlit). The ground, the hills across the lake and the planks are lit and the unlit picture's flatness is gone: the hills have light faces toward the sun (upper left) and dark faces away from it where the unlit hills are one grey-green, the dirt patch is warmer and lighter than unlit (luminance 97 against 85), the planks' tops are lighter than their sides and the posts have a light and a dark side. Against the emulated frame it is **darker throughout** (mean luminance of the client area 55 against 106; the dirt 97 against 143, the planks 50 against 63, the hills 63 against 178, where the emulated hills are mostly haze) and it has no sky, no haze and no water colour. **Too bright where the game has shadow**: the emulated planks carry the tree's shadow as dark patches and the lit planks are evenly lit; the same for the ground under the tree at the left. **Unlit things stand out**: the tree's trunk at the left reads 85 against the emulated 62 (1.39 times; it is the unlit texture) while the lit planks beside it are below the emulated ones, so the trunk, the leaves and the plants look pasted on a darker scene; the hero and the dog likewise.
- *The dark gaps between the planks* (asked about in the review of the shots). Measured in a rectangle of the deck without the hero (`real_measures.txt`): luminance at the 5th / 50th / 95th percentile is 20.5 / 53.0 / 68.6 lit, 23.6 / 66.3 / 99.0 emulated, 44.0 / 57.3 / 71.0 unlit. So the gaps of the lit picture are as dark as the game's own (20.5 against 23.6) and it is the unlit picture that had them too light; what the lit picture lacks is the brightness of the tops (68.6 against 99.0). Where the darkness comes from (`plank_sides.py` on run 314's capture, all 120 lit objects, 295,717 vertices, albedo taken as 1): a face the sun is not on has no sun term and an ambient of 0.16 to 0.19 (displayed 0.40 to 0.43 of the albedo), against 0.79 (0.89) for an upward face in the sun and 0.49 (0.70) for an upright face in the sun. It is **not the ambient at its floor**: on the averted faces `t` is 0.87 to 0.97 and the factor `L` 0.79 to 0.95 (the floor is (0.059, 0.09, 0)). The ambient is small because the baked term is small: the colour stream's luminance is 0.39 to 0.50, through the power 2.2 it is 0.14 to 0.23, and times the tint and `L` it gives those values. Two facts of the capture seen on the way: the frame's ambient axis `c23` equals the sun's direction (to three places, in this frame), so "turned from the ambient axis" and "turned from the sun" are the same faces; and every lit object of the frame has `c27.w` = 1, `c49` = `c26` = (1, 1, 1).
- *Third spot* (`task7r_third_shot_48s.png`; unlit: Task 6's `task6f_third_shot_50s.png`; sheets `real_third_*.png`). The same kinds of difference: planks with dark gaps and lighter tops (5th / 50th / 95th percentile 20.2 / 55.3 / 69.2 lit, 23.5 / 79.1 / 105.1 emulated, 44.8 / 57.6 / 69.6 unlit), rocky hills with lit and averted faces, the stockade's poles with a dark side. **Wrong to the eye here**: two pale, almost white mountains at the left and right top edge. They are the same pixels as in Task 6's shot (0 of 2,750 differ in a rectangle on the left one), so they are draws this task does not light, and against the darker lit ground they are now the brightest thing in the picture (luminance 153 against the lit hills' 73). The lake's plane is darker than unlit (94 against 99) here and unchanged at the bridge.
- *Trees* (`task7r_trees_shot_62s.png`; unlit: `task6f_trees_shot_64s.png`; sheets `real_trees_*.png`). Most of this picture is leaves, ferns and trunks, which are unlit (the leaves' rectangle differs from Task 6's shot in 2.3% of its pixels by more than 8 levels), so the picture as a whole changes least: client-area luminance 68 lit, 71 unlit, 74 emulated. What is lit shows it: the posts' tops are light and their sides dark (tops 97, sides 32; emulated 154 and 33; unlit 108 and 49), and the stone wall at the right, which faces away from the sun, is 46 against 76 unlit and 58 emulated: darker than the game's. The ground is hardly visible under the plants. The trunks read 90 against the emulated 76.
- *Split and overlay* (bridge `50s`, `56s`; the others likewise): the lit half continues the emulated half's planks, rails, posts and shoreline across the seam without a step in place; the step is in brightness and in the missing sky.
- *The emulated shots around each native shot* (`real_measures.txt`): before against after differ by 10.0 levels on average at the bridge (24% of the client area over 8 levels), 31.2 at the third spot (48%) and 17.1 by the trees (57%): the quest banner over the top third of the earlier shot at two of the spots, moving leaves and their shadows, clouds and the hero. They bracket the native shot in time, they are not a still reference; the figures above use the earlier one.
- *The dirt patch against the replay* (`dirt_native.py`, `dirt_native.txt`, `dirt_native_sheet.png`; run 314's capture, frame 2, square 30 replayed at 192 x 192; every replay pixel is projected into the shot with the row's `c0..c3` at the heightmap's height and the 1,279 that fall into the clear dirt rectangle of the shots are compared with the pixel there). Native lit (62 s, 2 s before the capture) over lit replay: **0.979, 0.978, 0.969** as the ratio of the means in red, green and blue (per point: median 0.98, 0.98, 0.97, tenth to ninetieth percentile 0.80 to 1.22: the native texture is filtered, the replay takes one texel). The control without light (`dirt_shade.py`, `dirt_shade.txt`): the unlit statement shows the colour times its facet shade, and that shade has to be computed for this ground, not assumed. From the capture's `c0..c3`, the heights and the shader's own `ndc = (c.xy / |c.w|, 0.01 c.w)`, over the 31 grid triangles the rectangle's points lie on, it is **0.950** (0.944 to 0.957); 0.924 is the value of a face whose `w` does not change over the screen, which this ground seen at a slant is not. Native unlit (run 317) over unlit replay times the shade: **0.982, 0.983, 0.972**. So the native ground is 2% (3% in blue) below the replay **with the light off as with it on**: the deficit is in the material path the two have in common, or in the comparison, and is older than the light. The light itself agrees: native lit over native unlit is 1.127, 1.172, 1.153 and lit replay over shaded unlit replay 1.130, 1.178, 1.156, the two within 0.5% of each other in every channel. (A first version of this paragraph took the shade as 0.924, read the control as agreeing to 1% and so put about 3% on the lit path: wrong, corrected in fix round 1 after the review, which also showed that a replay with its detail textures box-filtered over 8 and 16 texels gives the same ratios, so mip filtering is not it.) The 2% is **more than one store's rounding** (at most 0.8% of a display value in red and green and 1.6% in blue); the truncation of the 11 / 11 / 10 bit target at each of the six blended stores is a plausible share and was not measured. It is listed with the materials picture's follow-ups (section 13, item 13). Emulated over lit replay: 1.44, 1.41, 1.33 (36 s) and 1.45, 1.42, 1.34 (44 s); emulated over native lit 1.47, 1.44, 1.37. That is the uncalibrated exposure: a factor of about 1.4 as displayed, 2.0 in linear terms, a little less in blue.
- *Cost* (same display state as Task 6's final runs 304 to 306; `[native] frames:` windows of 150 frames, native view, after the scene has loaded): bridge 25.0, 24.7, 25.3, 25.4, 25.3 fps with the light on against 26.2, 26.3, 25.8, 26.1, 25.8 with it off (run 317) and 25.1, 26.4, 25.5, 26.9, 26.1 in run 304: **about 0.7 fps (3%) for the light at the bridge**; third spot 26.1, 23.6, 24.4, 24.6, 24.8 against run 306's 26.7, 24.1, 25.0, 24.7, 24.8, 24.6, 24.8; trees 28.8 to 29.8 against 28.8 to 29.2 (at the frame cap). The view-off windows are 28.6 to 30.1 in all runs. The clay line's CPU times are within their run-to-run spread (bridge: hash 1.11, decode 0.52, record 0.73 ms lit; 0.82, 0.43, 0.53 unlit; 0.90, 0.76, 0.55 in run 304; largest `max total` of a window without a view switch 6.0 ms lit, 5.0 unlit, 6.6 in run 304). Geometry resident 34.0 MB at the bridge (34.0 in run 304; the light's buffers were already built since Task 5a), 42.8 at the third spot (42.7), 70.7 by the trees (69.8). Textures resident 193 (26.0 MB) at the bridge against 189 (25.7 MB) with the light off and 187 (25.5 MB) in run 304; the light adds the base maps (the bridge frame names five: one of 768 x 768 for 551 records and four small ones; the counts are of each run's last window and do not differ by exactly five). Texture sets of the terrain 86, 89, 33 as in Task 6 (the base map is the same for all the records that share a set); pipelines 13, 13, 15 as in Task 6. The constant buffer holds one slot for the frame, one per terrain record and one per lit object: 685 slots of 256 bytes at the bridge (175,360 bytes of the 196,608 it had before), 690 at the third spot, 567 by the trees.
- *The light is not still within a run.* The game's sun and its colours move with its clock: the lit dirt reads 1.8% darker at 40 s than at 62 s of run 314 (113.0 against 115.1 in red), the `Light:` lines of one run name different sun directions, and Task 5b measured that two lit shots of one run 16 s apart differ by a level or more in 60% of their pixels. So only the native shot nearest a capture is compared with that capture's replay (62 s for the capture at 64 s above), and lit shots of two runs are compared by statistics over regions, never pixel for pixel; pixel for pixel holds only with the light off.
- *After fix round 1* (binary of 2026-10-08 07:15:48: the index rule's low end and the frame without constants, above; runs 322 bridge and 323 bridge with the light off, 07:25 to 07:28, the controlling agent's, `RUN UNCHECKED`, exit 0; `task7f1_*`, `task7\fix1_lit_regions.txt`, `fix1_off_compare.txt`). No device removal and no `constant buffer could not be made` line; `skipped_bad_index 0 other 0` in every clay line of both runs (the new low end skips no record of this scene); `lit 684 (terrain-layer 492, terrain-far 72, object 120, ...)` with `unlit` 233 to 245, Terrain 564 of 564, 13 pipelines, 86 texture sets, as run 318 before the round. Light off: run 323's native shots at 42 s and 74 s against run 321's and against run 304's: **0 pixels differ outside what moves** in all four pairs. Light on against run 318, by regions: the mean luminance of the client area, the deck, the dirt, the hills, the far hill, the lake, the grass, the trunk and the leaves is the same to 0.1 of a level at 40 s and at 62 s (ratios 1.000), the deck's percentiles 20.5 / 53.0 / 68.5 against 20.5 / 53.0 / 68.6. Seen on the way: the two runs' shots of one plan second are nearly the same picture (mean difference 0.1 level, 0.2% of the pixels over 8 levels: the boot save starts the game's clock at the same time), while inside either run the shots at 40 s and 62 s differ by a level or more in 62% of the pixels; the rule above (regions, not pixels, between lit runs) stays, since nothing guarantees the first. Native-view windows after loading: 25.8, 25.5, 26.8, 26.4, 25.9 fps lit (run 318: 25.9, 26.0, 25.6, 27.3, 27.3) and 26.4, 26.0, 27.8, 25.4, 27.7, 27.6 unlit (run 321: 25.2, 25.3, 26.2, 26.2, 26.0): inside the spread of the windows.
- *Not judged*: motion (the shots are stills; no flicker test was made of a square becoming lit when its base map arrives), other times of day, a scene without a sun, interiors, the lanterns.

**Not there, by design or yet.** Shadows (the screen mask is 1: the ground under the tree at the bridge and everything by the trees is lit as if in the open); fog; normal maps; specular, emissive and cube terms; the lanterns; the light of probe-colour objects, trunks, plants, leaves, characters, fur and water. The clamp of `A` at 1 where an albedo divided by its alpha exceeds it. A frame's light when the base map of a square is still uploading (the square is unlit for those frames).

## Pending

1. **Pitch ablation** (done 2026-10-01, results in section 4; kept for re-runs). One run per significant pitch `<p>` (1120, 1040, 320, 1280, 560, 280):
```
cd out\build\win-amd64-release
& .\fable_2.exe --fullscreen=false --native_render_suppress_debug=true --native_render_suppress_pitches=<p>
```
   Record what disappears or breaks on screen and name the pass (shadow map, main scene, water, post, UI). Fill section 4 with `pitch | draws | observed effect when suppressed | pass name`. Suppression debug mode also fakes all occlusion queries, so objects that are normally culled may appear.
2. **Tiling re-capture** (done 2026-10-01, results in section 3b; kept for re-runs). The counters and hooks are in place: census rows now carry `gpu.bin_selects` (SET_BIN_SELECT* writes), `gpu.tiles` and `gpu.tile_selects` (distinct non-zero bin-select values; LO/HI half-writes can add an intermediate value), `gpu.pred_draws` (predicated draw packets executed), `gpu.pred_skips` (predicated packets skipped) and `gpu.extents` (per pitch: max window-scissor corner, i.e. tile size in EDRAM, and max viewport size). The 15 extra hooks from section 7 item 1 come from `tools\xdk_sigmatch\fable2_extra_hooks.json` (`0x822A6318` is skipped: `src/diagnostics/fps_probe.h` already overrides it). Regenerate with:
```
python tools\xdk_sigmatch\gen_census_hooks.py --map docs\native-renderer\xdk-map.json --init generated\default\fable_2_init.cpp --src src --out src\native\capture\xdk_hooks.inc --ids-out src\native\capture\xdk_hook_ids.h --extra tools\xdk_sigmatch\fable2_extra_hooks.json
```
   Re-run the capture procedure below in the world and summarize; the summary gains a Tiling section and an extents table. Menu smoke run (2026-10-01): predicated tiling is active even in the menu (about 11 bin-select writes, 181 predicated draws and 130 skipped predicated packets per frame).
3. **F3 attribution** (user, in the world). Run `fable_2.exe --fullscreen=false --vsync=false --guest_vblank_pacing=false`, open F3, and record the Guest line, the GPU emu line and the Verdict. Fills the gameplay attribution in section 5.

Capture procedure, for re-runs: from `out\build\win-amd64-release`, set `FABLE2_D3D_CENSUS=900` and `FABLE2_D3D_CENSUS_DELAY=<seconds until in the world>`, run `fable_2.exe`, wait for `[census] complete` in the game log, then clear both variables. Summarize with `python tools\xdk_sigmatch\summarize_census.py <capture> [--from-frame N --to-frame N]`.
